\begin{lemma}[Equivalence data and component transport]\label{lem:extension-equivalence-component-transport}
Let
\[
(\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r),
\qquad
(\mathfrak E'_r,\Band',\epsilon'_r)
\]
be banded stack lifts of the component sheaf on $\mathcal C_a$. Suppose an equivalence of stacks
\[
\Phi:\mathfrak E_r\to\mathfrak E'_r
\]
comes with a band isomorphism $\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'$ and satisfies
\[
\epsilon'_r\circ\pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r.
\]
Then, for every sheafified global section $v$, $\Phi$ restricts to an equivalence of full component substacks
\[
\Phi_v:\mathfrak E_r[v]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak E'_r[v],
\]
and this restricted equivalence is banded by $\theta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Transport of Giraud classes under banded equivalence]\label{lem:giraud-class-transport}
Let $\mathfrak G$ be a $\Band$-banded gerbe and let $\mathfrak G'$ be a $\Band'$-banded gerbe on the same site $\mathcal C$. Suppose
\[
\Phi:\mathfrak G\to\mathfrak G'
\]
is an equivalence of stacks whose induced maps on automorphism sheaves identify the bands through an isomorphism
\[
\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'.
\]
Then
\[
\theta_*[\mathfrak G]=[\mathfrak G']
\qquad\text{in}\qquad
H^2(\mathcal C,\Band').
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{theorem}[Canonical component-section obstruction map under banded stack lifts]\label{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let
\[
(\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r)
\]
be a $\Band$-banded stack lift of the component sheaf in the sense of \Cref{def:banded-stack-lift}. Then the assignment
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}:\Vis_{p,s}(r)\longrightarrow H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band),
\qquad
v\longmapsto [\mathfrak E_r[v]]
\]
is canonically defined, where $[\mathfrak E_r[v]]$ denotes the Giraud class of the $v$-component substack. More precisely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every sheafified global section $v$, the full substack $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is a $\Band$-banded gerbe on $\mathcal C_a$;
\item for every sheafified global section $v$,
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak E_r[v]\ \text{is neutral}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak E_r[v](a)\neq\varnothing;
\]
\item if
\[
(\mathfrak E'_r,\Band',\epsilon'_r)
\]
is another banded stack lift of the component sheaf and
\[
\Phi:\mathfrak E_r\to \mathfrak E'_r
\]
is an equivalence of stacks whose induced map on automorphism sheaves identifies the bands through an isomorphism
\[
\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'
\]
and whose map on connected components satisfies
\[
\epsilon'_r\circ \pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r,
\]
then for every sheafified global section $v$ the functor $\Phi$ restricts to an equivalence
\[
\mathfrak E_r[v]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak E'_r[v]
\]
and
\[
\theta_*\bigl(\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)\bigr)=\delta_{\mathfrak E'_r}(v)
\quad\text{in}\quad
H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band');
\]
\item if $r$ admits a gluing-sensitive banded lift datum and
\[
\mathfrak E_r=\mathfrak L_r=a(\mathfrak P_r)
\]
is its stackification, then
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=\omega_v
\]
for every global component section $v$ for which \textup{(H4)} holds.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Supporting component gerbes of global component sections]\label{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition}
In the pullback-based setting of \Cref{def:component-gerbes}, and using only the banded groupoid lift and component data fixed there, every global component section $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$ determines a full substack $\mathfrak G_v\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ that is a banded $\Band$-gerbe over $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$. In particular, neither essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre over $a$ nor the derived comparison hypothesis \textup{(H4)} is used here.
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[\v{C}ech obstruction of a component gerbe on an acyclic cover]\label{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction}
Assume that $\mathfrak G_v$ is a banded component gerbe with band $\Band$ in the sense of \Cref{def:component-gerbes}. Using only this gerbe and a single acyclic cover of $a$, let
\[
\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\in \Cov_{p,s}(a)
\]
be any cover whose \v{C}ech nerve exists in $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$, and write
\[
U_{i_0\cdots i_n}
\]
for the corresponding simplices. Assume that the component gerbe is locally nonempty on the cover, so that objects
\[
x_i\in \mathfrak G_v(U_i)
\]
exist for all $i$, and assume
\[
H^1(U_{i_0\cdots i_n},\Band)=0
\]
for every nonempty intersection used below, including all members, pairwise overlaps, triple overlaps, and quadruple overlaps appearing in the descent calculation.
Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every pair $(i,j)$ there exists an isomorphism
\[
\theta_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim} x_j|_{U_{ij}};
\]
\item on each triple overlap $U_{ijk}$, the element
\[
c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{jk}|_{U_{ijk}}\circ \theta_{ij}|_{U_{ijk}}
\in \Band(U_{ijk})
\]
defines a \v{C}ech $2$-cocycle $c=(c_{ijk})\in \check Z^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$;
\item the class $[c]\in \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band)$ depends only on the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ and the chosen cover: changing the local lift objects or the comparison isomorphisms changes $c$ by a \v{C}ech coboundary, and refinements of acyclic covers pull this class back functorially;
\item the class $[c]$ vanishes if and only if the component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is neutral over $a$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{lemma}[\v{C}ech calculus for component gerbes]\label{lem:cech-branch-gerbe-calculus}
In the setting of \Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction}, the following four operations have the indicated effects on the cocycle
\[
c_{ijk}:=\theta_{ik}^{-1}\theta_{jk}\theta_{ij}.
\]
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item If the local lift objects $x_i$ are fixed and $\theta'_{ij}=\theta_{ij}\circ h_{ij}^{-1}$ for a $1$-cochain $h=(h_{ij})$ with values in $\Band$, then the new cocycle is
\[
c'_{ijk}=h_{ik}\,c_{ijk}\,h_{jk}^{-1}h_{ij}^{-1};
\]
in additive notation, $c'-c=\delta h$.
\item If local lift objects $x_i$ are replaced by $x_i'$ and $\psi_i:x_i\xrightarrow{\sim}x_i'$ are chosen, then
\[
\theta'_{ij}:=\psi_j\theta_{ij}\psi_i^{-1}
\]
produces the same cocycle under the band identifications. Changing the $\psi_i$ modifies the result only by a coboundary of the kind in \textup{(i)}.
\item If $\lambda:\mathcal V\to\mathcal U$ is a refinement satisfying the same acyclicity hypotheses, the cocycle constructed from the pulled-back objects and isomorphisms on $\mathcal V$ is $\lambda^*c$.
\item The class $[c]$ is zero if and only if the descent datum may be adjusted so that the comparison isomorphisms satisfy the strict cocycle condition; by effective descent for the stack $\mathfrak G_v$, this is equivalent to a global object of $\mathfrak G_v(a)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proposition}[Derived component classes detect neutrality]\label{prop:derived-branch-classification}
Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H4)}. For every global component section $v$,
\[
\omega_v=0\ \text{in}\ H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Matching families versus global component sections]\label{prop:compsecs-vs-visible-branches}
Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admissible at $p$, admits a banded groupoid lift in the sense of \suppresult{Banded groupoid lift and stackification}, and that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice. Then global component sections detect the existence of matching families, without any terminal-fibre essential-surjectivity or derived-comparison hypothesis:
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{lemma}[Terminal-fibre nonemptiness and neutrality]\label{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}
Let $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$. Then
\[
\mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is neutral}.
\]
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_terminal_fibre_neutrality}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proposition}[Global sections versus neutral component gerbes]\label{prop:global-sections-vs-neutral-components}
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_global_sections_vs_neutral_components}
Assume that $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ is admissible at $p$, that it admits a banded groupoid lift in the sense of \suppresult{Banded groupoid lift and stackification}, and that this prestack is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$ in the sense of \suppresult{Essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre}. Then a global lift object exists exactly when some global component section has a global object:
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\exists v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\ \text{such that}\ \mathfrak G_v(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
Equivalently, by \Cref{lem:terminal-fibre-neutrality}, a global lift object exists exactly when at least one global component section gerbe is neutral.
\end{proposition}
\begin{theorem}[Component-gerbe criterion for matching without amalgamation]\label{thm:gerbe-null-semantics}
Assume \textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H3)} for an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at $p$, and assume in addition that \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is separated on the slice. Then matching without amalgamation is equivalent to the existence of at least one global component section and the non-neutrality of every component gerbe:
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
\ \text{and}\
\mathfrak G_v\ \text{is non-neutral for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r).
\]
If, in addition, \textup{(H4)} holds, then this is equivalently
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\neq\varnothing
\ \text{and}\
\omega_v\neq 0\ \text{in }H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)
\text{ for every }v\in\Vis_{p,s}(r).
\]
The companion theorem \cite[Theorem~5.20]{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} is the meet-semilattice analogue of this pullback-site formulation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Pseudofunctorial assignment of $K_\omega$ and $Q_\omega$]\label{prop:invariance-visible-quotients}
Let $(\mathcal U,A,\omega)$ and $(\mathcal U',A',\omega')$ be finite nerve representatives of the same component obstruction class. Suppose first that the two presentations differ by one of the following elementary comparisons:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item refinement of the cover by a simplicial map
\[
f:N(\mathcal U')\to N(\mathcal U)
\]
from the refined nerve to the original nerve, such that
\[
\omega'=f^*\omega
\]
and
\[
f_*:H_2(N(\mathcal U'),\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\sim} H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)
\]
is an isomorphism, with the coefficient group fixed;
\item replacement of the \v{C}ech cocycle by a cohomologous cocycle in $H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$;
\item re-trivialization of the band by an isomorphism of coefficient groups
\[
\theta:A\xrightarrow{\sim} A',
\]
so that
\[
\omega'=\theta_*\omega.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Then there exists a specified coefficient comparison isomorphism $\phi:A\to A'$---the identity in \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)}, and $\theta$ in \textup{(iii)}---such that
\[
\phi\bigl(K_{\omega}\bigr)=K_{\omega'}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\bar\phi:Q_{\omega}=A/K_\omega\xrightarrow{\sim}A'/K_{\omega'}=Q_{\omega'}
\]
is the induced quotient isomorphism. In particular, both $K_{\omega}$ and $Q_{\omega}$ are transported by the three elementary comparison operations listed above, with the refinement direction fixed by the displayed pullback map.

More generally, if the two presentations are joined by a morphism $\Gamma$ in
the presentation-comparison groupoid of
\Cref{def:finite-nerve-comparison-relation}, then the chosen finite comparison
zigzag $\Gamma$ determines, by composition of the elementary coefficient
comparisons and their inverses, an isomorphism
\[
\phi_\Gamma:A\xrightarrow{\sim} A'
\]
such that
\[
\phi_\Gamma(K_\omega)=K_{\omega'}
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
A/K_\omega\xrightarrow{\sim}A'/K_{\omega'}
\]
is the corresponding composite quotient isomorphism. Hence
$(\mathcal U,A,\omega)\mapsto (K_\omega\le A)$ and
$(\mathcal U,A,\omega)\mapsto Q_\omega$ define a pseudofunctorial assignment over the
presentation-comparison groupoid. They are invariants of a chosen presentation:
once a finite nerve, coefficient trivialization, and comparison data have
been fixed, the subgroup and quotient are determined by that object. Along a
chosen comparison zigzag they are transported functorially by the displayed
composite isomorphism. The statement does not assert path independence, and
it does not identify the isomorphisms attached to two different zigzags with
the same endpoints.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Finite nerve comparison isomorphism]\label{prop:finite-nerve-comparison}
Work in one finite nerve representative
$(\mathcal U,A,\omega)$ of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}; in
particular, the band has been trivialized on $\mathcal U$ and is being
viewed as the constant finite abelian group $A$ on the selected finite
nerve. Use the total order on the finite index set $I$ already fixed in
\suppresult{Ordered \v{C}ech convention} for
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}.
\]
Use the ordered \v{C}ech convention in which $\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)$
is indexed by tuples
\[
i_0<\cdots<i_n
\]
with nonempty overlap, and use the corresponding ordered oriented
simplices of $N(\mathcal U)$ for simplicial cochains. For each $n\ge 0$,
each nonempty ordered $(n{+}1)$-fold overlap $a_{i_0\cdots i_n}$
determines the oriented $n$-simplex $(i_0,\dots,i_n)$ of the selected
nerve, and the assignment
\[
\Theta_{\mathcal U}^n:\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)\to C^n(N(\mathcal U),A),\qquad
(\alpha_{i_0\cdots i_n})\longmapsto\bigl((i_0,\dots,i_n)\mapsto \alpha_{i_0\cdots i_n}\bigr)
\]
is an isomorphism of cochain groups. Under this correspondence,
\[
\delta\leftrightarrow d,\qquad
B^n\leftrightarrow B^n,\qquad
Z^n\leftrightarrow Z^n.
\]
Hence the \v{C}ech cohomology of this selected ordered finite nerve
presentation with constant coefficients $A$ is identified with the
simplicial cohomology of $N(\mathcal U)$ with coefficients in $A$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Chain-coordinate versus homological image subgroups]\label{prop:strict-vs-intrinsic-hidden-subgroups}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, let $\alpha\in Z^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$ be a simplicial cocycle representing $\omega$, and let
\[
\widetilde\alpha:C_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
be the chain-level evaluation map obtained by linear extension from the oriented $2$-simplices. Then
\[
H_\alpha=\operatorname{Im}(\widetilde\alpha)
\]
and
\[
K_{\omega}:=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}).
\]
Thus $H_\alpha$ is the chain-coordinate image: it is generated by the values of the chosen cocycle on all $2$-chains. By contrast, $K_{\omega}$ is the homological image obtained after passage to $2$-homology. In particular,
\[
K_{\omega}\subseteq H_\alpha.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Character-theoretic detection of the homological cokernel]\label{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:class-admissible-character},
\[
X_{\omega}
=
\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi\circ \mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0\}
=
\mathrm{Ann}(K_{\omega}).
\]
Consequently,
\[
K_{\omega}
=
\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi,
\qquad
Q_{\omega}\cong A\Big/\!\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Character detection of the homological cokernel]\label{cor:intrinsic-character-detection}
The quotient map
\[
\pi_{\omega}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\omega}
\]
induces a natural isomorphism
\[
\widehat{Q_{\omega}}\cong X_{\omega}.
\]
For elements $x,y\in A$, one has
\[
\pi_{\omega}(x)=\pi_{\omega}(y)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\chi(x)=\chi(y)\ \text{for every class-trivializing }\chi.
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{theorem}[Initiality among zero-evaluation quotients]\label{thm:intrinsic-pure-ext-initiality}
In the finite nerve representative of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, let
\[
\pi_\omega:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_\omega=A/K_\omega
\]
be the homological cokernel. For every surjective homomorphism $q:A\twoheadrightarrow B$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $q$ factors uniquely through $\pi_\omega$;
\item $q$ kills $\operatorname{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_\omega)=K_\omega$;
\item $\eta_B(q_*\omega)=0$;
\item $q_*(\omega)$ lies in the UCT $\operatorname{Ext}^1$ subgroup
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),B)
\subseteq H^2(N(\mathcal U),B)
\]
of the universal coefficient sequence.
\end{enumerate}
Thus $Q_\omega$ is initial among coefficient quotients for which the pushed-forward obstruction has zero $H_2$ evaluation. This is the pullback-site, finite-nerve form of the companion initiality theorem \cite[Theorem~6.13]{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Class-trivializing initiality when $H_1$ vanishes]\label{cor:h1-vanishing-class-trivializing}
Assume
\[
H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Then, for every surjective homomorphism $q:A\twoheadrightarrow B$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $q_*(\omega)=0$ in $H^2(N(\mathcal U),B)$;
\item $q$ factors uniquely through
\[
\pi_{\omega}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\omega}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Hence, when $H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0$, the homological cokernel is initial among coefficient quotients that annihilate the obstruction class itself.
\end{corollary}
\begin{corollary}[Comparison with cocycle-coordinate quotients]\label{cor:strict-to-intrinsic-visible}
Let $\alpha$ be any finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative of the class $\omega$. Then the homological cokernel
\[
Q_{\omega}
\]
admits a canonical surjective homomorphism onto the cocycle-coordinate quotient
\[
A/H_\alpha.
\]
Equivalently, every cocycle-coordinate quotient is a quotient of the homological cokernel.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proposition}[Chain-coordinate versus homology-evaluation quotient]\label{prop:chain-vs-homological-visibility}
In the setting of \Cref{def:chain-level-evaluation}, the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\widetilde\alpha$ vanishes on $2$-boundaries and therefore induces a homomorphism
\[
\overline\alpha:H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)\to A.
\]
\item The induced homomorphism $\overline\alpha$ is exactly the homological evaluation map $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}$.
\item The strict coordinate-image subgroup and the presentation-relative homological image subgroup satisfy
\[
H_\alpha=\mathrm{Im}(\widetilde\alpha),
\qquad
K_{\omega}=\mathrm{Im}(\overline\alpha)=\widetilde\alpha(Z_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)).
\]
\end{enumerate}
Thus the gap between chain-coordinate quotient and homology-evaluation quotient is exactly the gap between the image of the cocycle on all $2$-chains and its image on $2$-cycles.
\end{proposition}
\begin{corollary}[Coincidence of the chain-coordinate and homology-evaluation quotients]\label{cor:strict-intrinsic-coincidence-criterion}
Let $\alpha$ be a finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative of $\omega$. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $A/H_\alpha\cong Q_{\omega}$;
\item $H_\alpha=K_{\omega}$;
\item $\widetilde\alpha(C_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z))=\widetilde\alpha(Z_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z))$.
\end{enumerate}
Equivalently, the chain-coordinate quotient agrees with the homology-evaluation quotient exactly when every value of the cocycle on a $2$-chain already occurs on a $2$-cycle.
\end{corollary}
\begin{theorem}[Nonzero UCT-Ext classes and vanishing $H_2$ evaluation]\label{thm:character-blind-obstructions}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, with a chosen admissible
parameter, global component section, banded groupoid lift, trivialized constant finite
abelian band $A$, and the \textup{(H4)} comparison datum needed to regard
$\omega$ as a derived component class, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $K_{\omega}=0$;
\item $Q_{\omega}=A$;
\item $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0$;
\item $\omega$ lies in the subgroup
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\subseteq H^2(N(\mathcal U),A).
\]
\end{enumerate}
If these conditions hold and $\omega\neq 0$, then the chosen component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is non-neutral, while every character of $A$ is class-trivializing for that component class. If, in addition, the banded groupoid lift is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre, \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is separated on the slice, and $|\Vis_{p,s}(r)|=1$, then
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Vanishing evaluation when $H_2=0$]\label{cor:h2-vanishing-blindness}
Assume
\[
H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Then, for every obstruction class $\omega\in H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)$,
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0,
\qquad
K_{\omega}=0,
\qquad
Q_{\omega}=A.
\]
Every nonzero component obstruction class on such a nerve therefore has zero $H_2$ evaluation, and every character is class-trivializing.
\end{corollary}
\begin{corollary}[No nonzero classes with vanishing $H_2$ evaluation when $H_1$ is free]\label{cor:h1-free-no-blindness}
Assume that $H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)$ is free abelian. Then
\[
K_{\omega}=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\omega=0.
\]
Equivalently, every nonzero component obstruction class has nonzero $H_2$ evaluation. In particular, if $|\Vis_{p,s}(r)|=1$, every nonzero component obstruction class on such a nerve produces a proper homological cokernel.
\end{corollary}
\begin{corollary}[Fibre size of the homological quotient]\label{cor:intrinsic-hidden-state-lower-bound}
Every auxiliary register for the homological quotient satisfies
\[
|R|\ge |K_{\omega}|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2|R|\ge \log_2|K_{\omega}|.
\]
This lower bound is sharp.
\end{corollary}
\begin{theorem}[Prime decomposition of the homological quotient and fibre size]\label{thm:intrinsic-prime-decomposition}
Write the finite abelian group $A$ as a direct sum of its $p$-primary components:
\[
A=\bigoplus_p A_{(p)}.
\]
Let $\omega_p\in H^2(N(\mathcal U),A_{(p)})$ be the image of $\omega$ under the projection onto the $p$-primary summand. Then
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=\bigoplus_p \mathrm{ev}_{\omega_p},
\qquad
K_{\omega}=\bigoplus_p K_{\omega,p},
\qquad
Q_{\omega}\cong \bigoplus_p A_{(p)}/K_{\omega,p},
\]
where
\[
K_{\omega,p}:=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_p}).
\]
Consequently,
\[
|K_{\omega}|=\prod_p |K_{\omega,p}|,
\qquad
\log_2|K_{\omega}|=\sum_p \log_2|K_{\omega,p}|.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Homological versus chain-coordinate fibre size]\label{cor:strict-intrinsic-budget-comparison}
For every finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative $\alpha$ of the class $\omega$,
\[
|K_{\omega}|\le |H_\alpha|,
\qquad
\log_2|K_{\omega}|\le \log_2|H_\alpha|.
\]
Hence the minimum auxiliary register size for the homological quotient never exceeds that for the cocycle-coordinate quotient. Equality holds if and only if the canonical surjection
\[
Q_{\omega}\twoheadrightarrow A/H_\alpha
\]
is an isomorphism.
\end{corollary}
\begin{corollary}[Surjective homological evaluation]\label{cor:maximal-collapse}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}:H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)\to A$ is surjective;
\item $K_{\omega}=A$;
\item $Q_{\omega}=0$;
\item the logarithmic fibre cardinality of the homological quotient is $\log_2|A|$:
\[
\log_2|K_{\omega}|=\log_2|A|.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\section{Component gerbes and component obstruction classes}\label{sec:gerbe-obstruction}

From this point on the argument proceeds componentwise relative to a chosen
banded groupoid lift. When derived obstruction classes are needed, the
\v{C}ech-to-derived comparison hypothesis below is also imposed. No unconditional
existence theorem for such data is sought here. This section reorganizes the
gerbe-theoretic obstruction data of the companion paper
\cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} in the language of banded stack
lifts used later. The first result of the section
isolates the invariance available once the sheafified presheaf
functor has been lifted to a banded stack: the component obstruction class is
invariant under equivalence of the resulting banded stack lifts. Since a
single parameter may carry several global component sections,
the relevant obstruction objects are the corresponding component gerbes
rather than a single global gerbe. The cocycle constructions used below are
the standard local-trivialization constructions for gerbes
\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Sec.~5]{Breen2006}, together with their
bundle-gerbe counterparts \cite{Murray1996,Brylinski1993}, applied
componentwise in the present setting.

We begin by fixing the hypotheses used in the section. Individual statements invoke only the subcollection they require.

\begin{definition}[\v{C}ech-to-derived comparison hypothesis]\label{def:cech-derived-comparison-package}
For an admissible parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) at \(p\), a band \(\Band\)
on \(\mathcal C_{p,s}/a\), and a global component section \(v\), a
\emph{\v{C}ech-to-derived comparison hypothesis} consists of a filtered cofinal
family
\[
\Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a)\subseteq \Cov_{p,s}(a)
\]
such that every cover \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}\) in the family has a
\v{C}ech nerve in the slice site, trivializes the band on all intersections
used in degrees \(0,1,2\), and satisfies the following comparison condition:
the canonical refinement map
\[
\varinjlim_{\mathcal U\in\Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a)}
\check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band)
\longrightarrow
H^2(\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,\Band)
\]
is an isomorphism. One way to verify this condition is the usual Leray
hypothesis: all nonempty finite intersections appearing in the family are
\(\Band\)-acyclic in every positive degree needed for the derived
comparison. The definition itself, however, records the comparison
isomorphism as the required assumption; the later theorems do not silently
infer derived \(H^2\) from a single \v{C}ech cocycle unless this hypothesis, or
the explicit finite good-cover Leray theorem of
\suppresult{Finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve, Leray comparison, and one-state admissible expansion}, is in force.
\end{definition}

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(H\arabic*)},nosep]
\item\label{hyp:admitted-prestack} An admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$ with $a=[r]_p$, equipped with a banded groupoid lift $\mathfrak P_r \to \mathcal C_a$ and band $\Band$ in the sense of \suppresult{Banded groupoid lift and stackification}.
\item\label{hyp:band-identification} The band $\Band$ is identified with a fixed abelian sheaf on the slice site $\mathcal C_{p,s}/a$.
\item\label{hyp:global-conservativity} The banded groupoid lift $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$ in the sense of \suppresult{Essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre}.
\item\label{hyp:cofinal-gerbe-adapted} For every global component section \(v\), a
\v{C}ech-to-derived comparison hypothesis in the sense of
\Cref{def:cech-derived-comparison-package} has been fixed.
\end{enumerate}

When a statement compares global component sections with the strict predicate
\(\CompSec_s\), or with \(\NoAmalg_s\) through that predicate,
it separately assumes that \(F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\) is separated on the slice.
Without this separatedness assumption global component sections are only
sheafified sections, and no strict compatible family is asserted.

\begin{remark}[\v{C}ech versus derived cohomology]\label{rem:cech-vs-derived}
Hypothesis \ref{hyp:cofinal-gerbe-adapted}, equivalently the hypothesis of
\Cref{def:cech-derived-comparison-package}, is the precise comparison
assumption governing the passage between \v{C}ech and derived $H^2$. It
requires the comparison isomorphism itself; acyclicity in positive degrees is
one standard sufficient Leray condition, not an unstated assumption unless
explicitly invoked. Without this hypothesis, the component obstruction class lives in
the filtered colimit
\[
\varinjlim_{\mathcal U\in \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a)} \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band),
\]
and the non-neutrality statement in \Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction} is purely \v{C}ech-theoretic. With \ref{hyp:cofinal-gerbe-adapted}, this colimit is canonically identified with the derived $H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band)$, and the obstruction class $\omega_v$ is an element of derived cohomology. All theorems in this subsection state explicitly whether they use only the \v{C}ech-level data or additionally require the comparison isomorphism.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the companion paper]
The companion paper \cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} formulates component obstructions relative to a chosen gluing-sensitive banded groupoid lift and its stackification. The present section isolates the three constructions needed later: the promotion of cover-relative \v{C}ech classes to derived classes $\omega_v$ under \textup{(H4)}, the reformulation of the obstruction theory for arbitrary banded stack lifts of the component sheaf of the sheafified presheaf of local sections, and the componentwise neutrality criteria stated directly in terms of component gerbes.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Component gerbes and component obstruction classes]\label{def:component-gerbes}\label{def:cech-gluing-obstruction}
Fix an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\]
and assume that $r$ admits a banded groupoid lift $\mathfrak P_r$ with stackification $\mathfrak L_r$ and band $\Band$ as in \suppresult{Banded groupoid lift and stackification}. For a global component section
\[
v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a),
\]
let $\mathfrak G_v(U)$ be the full subgroupoid of $\mathfrak L_r(U)$ on those objects whose connected-component class restricts to $v$ on $U$. When \Cref{thm:component-gerbe-decomposition} applies, we call $\mathfrak G_v$ the \emph{component gerbe} of the global component section $v$.

Assume moreover that \textup{(H4)} holds. For each global component section $v$ and each cover
\[
\mathcal U\in \Cov_v^{\mathrm{acyc}}(a),
\]
\Cref{thm:cech-component-gerbe-obstruction} produces a \v{C}ech obstruction class
\[
[g_{\mathcal U}]\in \check H^2(\mathcal U,\Band).
\]
Refinement compatibility identifies these classes in the filtered system, and the comparison isomorphism in \textup{(H4)} therefore determines a unique derived class
\[
\omega_v\in H^2(\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,\Band),
\]
called the \emph{component obstruction class} of $v$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Banded stack lift of the component sheaf]\label{def:banded-stack-lift}
For an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, abbreviate
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and fix an abelian sheaf $\Band$ on $\mathcal C_a$. Let $\mathfrak E_r$ be a
$\Band$-banded stack in groupoids on $\mathcal C_a$. A
\emph{$\Band$-banded stack lift of the component sheaf} is a pair
\[
(\mathfrak E_r,\epsilon_r)
\]
consisting of such a stack together with an isomorphism of sheaves
\[
\epsilon_r:\pi_0(\mathfrak E_r)\xrightarrow{\sim} a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\]
on $\mathcal C_a$.

For a sheafified global section
\[
v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a),
\]
the \emph{$v$-component substack} of $\mathfrak E_r$ is the full substack
\[
\mathfrak E_r[v](U):=
\bigl\{x\in \mathfrak E_r(U)\mid \epsilon_{r,U}([x])=v|_U\bigr\}
\]
for objects $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$, where $\epsilon_{r,U}$ denotes the component of $\epsilon_r$ at $U$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Equivalence data and component transport]\label{lem:extension-equivalence-component-transport}
Let
\[
(\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r),
\qquad
(\mathfrak E'_r,\Band',\epsilon'_r)
\]
be banded stack lifts of the component sheaf on $\mathcal C_a$. Suppose an equivalence of stacks
\[
\Phi:\mathfrak E_r\to\mathfrak E'_r
\]
comes with a band isomorphism $\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'$ and satisfies
\[
\epsilon'_r\circ\pi_0(\Phi)=\epsilon_r.
\]
Then, for every sheafified global section $v$, $\Phi$ restricts to an equivalence of full component substacks
\[
\Phi_v:\mathfrak E_r[v]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak E'_r[v],
\]
and this restricted equivalence is banded by $\theta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $U$ be an object of $\mathcal C_a$ and let $x\in\mathfrak E_r[v](U)$. By definition,
\[
\epsilon_{r,U}([x])=v|_U.
\]
The compatibility condition gives
\[
\epsilon'_{r,U}([\Phi(x)])
=
(\epsilon'_r\circ\pi_0(\Phi))_U([x])
=
\epsilon_{r,U}([x])
=
v|_U.
\]
Thus $\Phi(x)\in\mathfrak E'_r[v](U)$, so $\Phi$ restricts to a functor
\[
\Phi_v:\mathfrak E_r[v]\to\mathfrak E'_r[v].
\]
Since both component substacks are full, $\Phi_v$ is fully faithful because $\Phi$ is. For local essential surjectivity, take $z\in\mathfrak E'_r[v](U)$. A quasi-inverse $\Psi$ to $\Phi$ gives, after possibly refining $U$, an object $y\in\mathfrak E_r(U)$ with $\Phi(y)\cong z$. Applying $\epsilon'_r$ to this local isomorphism gives
\[
\epsilon_{r,U}([y])
=
\epsilon'_{r,U}([\Phi(y)])
=
\epsilon'_{r,U}([z])
=
v|_U,
\]
so $y$ lies locally in $\mathfrak E_r[v]$. Hence $\Phi_v$ is an equivalence of stacks. The automorphism-sheaf maps of $\Phi_v$ are the restrictions of the automorphism-sheaf maps of $\Phi$, so the band is transported by the same isomorphism $\theta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Transport of Giraud classes under banded equivalence]\label{lem:giraud-class-transport}
Let $\mathfrak G$ be a $\Band$-banded gerbe and let $\mathfrak G'$ be a $\Band'$-banded gerbe on the same site $\mathcal C$. Suppose
\[
\Phi:\mathfrak G\to\mathfrak G'
\]
is an equivalence of stacks whose induced maps on automorphism sheaves identify the bands through an isomorphism
\[
\theta:\Band\xrightarrow{\sim}\Band'.
\]
Then
\[
\theta_*[\mathfrak G]=[\mathfrak G']
\qquad\text{in}\qquad
H^2(\mathcal C,\Band').
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose a cover $\{U_i\to U\}$ which locally trivializes $\mathfrak G$, objects $x_i\in\mathfrak G(U_i)$, and descent isomorphisms
\[
\varphi_{ij}:x_i|_{U_{ij}}\xrightarrow{\sim}x_j|_{U_{ij}}.
\]
The usual Giraud cocycle $c_{ijk}\in\Band(U_{ijk})$ is defined by
\[
\varphi_{ik}^{-1}\varphi_{jk}\varphi_{ij}=c_{ijk}\in\operatorname{Aut}(x_i|_{U_{ijk}})\cong\Band(U_{ijk}).
\]
Applying $\Phi$ gives local lift objects $\Phi(x_i)$ of $\mathfrak G'$ and comparison isomorphisms $\Phi(\varphi_{ij})$. The corresponding defect is
\[
\Phi(\varphi_{ik})^{-1}\Phi(\varphi_{jk})\Phi(\varphi_{ij})
=
\Phi(c_{ijk}),
\]
which is identified with $\theta(c_{ijk})$ under the band isomorphism. Thus the transported local trivialization of $\mathfrak G'$ has cocycle $\theta_*(c)$. Refining the cover or changing the local lift objects changes both cocycles by the corresponding coboundary, exactly as in the standard local-trivialization classification of banded gerbes \cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}, \cite[Tag 06NY]{StacksProject}. Hence the cohomology class of $\mathfrak G'$ is $\theta_*[\mathfrak G]$.

The functoriality invoked here is the usual naturality of Giraud's classification with respect to a morphism of bands. Equivalently, the assignment
\[
(\mathfrak G,\Band)\longmapsto [\mathfrak G]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\Band)
\]
is functorial for banded equivalences: an equivalence of gerbes whose automorphism sheaves are identified by $\theta$ transports a local descent-defect cocycle to its pointwise image under $\theta$, and this construction is independent of all choices after passage to cohomology.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Canonical component-section obstruction map under banded stack lifts]\label{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let
\[
(\mathfrak E_r,\Band,\epsilon_r)
\]
be a $\Band$-banded stack lift of the component sheaf in the sense of \Cref{def:banded-stack-lift}. Then the assignment
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}:\Vis_{p,s}(r)\longrightarrow H^2(\mathcal C_a,\Band),
\qquad
v\longmapsto [\mathfrak E_r[v]]
\]
is canonically defined, where $[\mathfrak E_r[v]]$ denotes the Giraud class of the $v$-component substack. More precisely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every sheafified global section $v$, the full substack $\mathfrak E_r[v]$ is a $\Band$-banded gerbe on $\mathcal C_a$;
\item for every sheafified global section $v$,
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_r}(v)=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak E_r[v]\ \text{is neutral}

\begin{lemma}[Wedge-of-spheres good-cover and finite-abelian interfaces]\label{lem:wedge-sphere-cover-interface}
Let $K$ be a finite simplicial complex and let $\mathcal U^{(m)}$ be the open-star cover of $\operatorname{Sd}^m K$ on $|K|$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item every nonempty finite intersection of members of $\mathcal U^{(m)}$ is contractible, and $N(\mathcal U^{(m)})$ is canonically isomorphic to $\operatorname{Sd}^mK$;
\item the covers $\mathcal U^{(m+1)}$ refine $\mathcal U^{(m)}$, and in the pullback site generated by all such open stars, their nonempty intersections, and a strict initial object for empty intersections, the family $(\mathcal U^{(m)})_{m\ge0}$ is filtered and cofinal;
\item for every constant finite abelian group $G$, the Leray--\v{C}ech comparison identifies
\[
\varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},G)
\cong
H^2(|K|,G),
\]
and the comparison is compatible with the simplicial cohomology of the nerves;
\item for every integer $\beta\ge 1$ and every nonzero finite abelian group $G$, the
group $G$ admits a decomposition
\[
G=G_1\oplus G_2
\]
with $G_1,G_2$ nonzero and $d(G_i)\le\beta$ for $i=1,2$ if and only if $d(G)\le 2\beta$ and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Wedge-of-spheres mixed-component construction transfer]\label{lem:wedge-sphere-mixed-component-transfer}
Fix $\beta\ge 1$ and the open-star good-cover intersection site $\mathcal C_a$ built from
$K_\beta$ and the open-star covers $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$ as in
\Cref{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}. Let $G$ be a finite abelian
group, and let $G_1,G_2\le G$ be nonzero subgroups satisfying
\[
d(G_i)\le \beta\qquad(i=1,2).
\]
Then there exist a state $p$, an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$
inside a model $M$ with $a=[r]_p$, a presheaf of local sections on
$\mathcal C_a$, and a banded groupoid lift with constant band $G$ such
that
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v_1,v_2\},
\qquad
K_{v_i}=G_i\quad(i=1,2).
\]
Both component gerbes are non-neutral. If, moreover,
\[
G_1\cap G_2=0,\qquad G_1+G_2=G,
\]
then
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\cong G,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)=0,
\qquad
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{theorem}[Two-component label-loss kernels on chosen good covers of the wedge of $\beta$ $2$-spheres]\label{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}
Fix an integer $\beta\ge 1$, let $K_\beta$ be a finite connected simplicial complex whose geometric realization is homotopy equivalent to
\[
\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta} S^2,
\]
and write
\[
X_\beta:=|K_\beta|.
\]
For each $m\ge 0$, let
\[
\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}
\]
be the open-star cover of the barycentric subdivision $\operatorname{Sd}^m K_\beta$, viewed as a cover of $X_\beta$. Let $\mathcal C_a$ be the pullback site whose objects are $X_\beta$, a strict initial object $\emptyset$, and all nonempty finite intersections of members of the covers $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$; the pullback of two objects over a common target is their nonempty intersection when it is nonempty and is $\emptyset$ otherwise. Covering families are generated by the families $\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}$, their pullbacks, identity covers, and the empty cover of $\emptyset$.
All typed-expansion construction assertions in this theorem are understood relative to the
typed-expansion convention of
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}.

Then the family
\[
\bigl(\mathcal U_\beta^{(m)}\bigr)_{m\ge 0}
\]
is a filtered cofinal family satisfying \textup{(H4)} for every constant finite abelian band.

Let $G$ be a finite abelian group, and write $d(G)$ for the minimal number of generators of $G$. Then the following are equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $G\ne0$, $d(G)\le 2\beta$, and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group, where
cyclic $p$-group means cyclic of order $p^n$ with $n\ge1$.
\item In the typed-expansion model class generated by
\Cref{prop:admissible-layer-expansion-interface}, the one-state
\(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the site \(\mathcal C_a\), a presheaf of local sections on
\(\mathcal C_a\), and a banded groupoid lift
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_a
\]
with trivialized constant band $G$ define a one-state
\(\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3\) model \(M\) with state \(p\) and admissible
parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\), \(a=[r]_p\), using the finite-nerve Leray
comparison supplied by the chosen good covers of the wedge of $\beta$ $2$-spheres, such that
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v_1,v_2\},
\]
both component gerbes $\mathfrak G_{v_1}$ and $\mathfrak G_{v_2}$ are non-neutral, and
\[
K_{v_1}\cap K_{v_2}=0,
\qquad
K_{v_1}+K_{v_2}=G.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Whenever these conditions hold,
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\cong G,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)=0,
\qquad
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Label-aware quotient]\label{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, consider the diagonal map
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}}:A\longrightarrow \prod_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} Q_v,
\qquad
x\longmapsto (\pi_v(x))_v.
\]
Then
\[
\ker(\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}})=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
\]
Consequently, the quotient
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r):=A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
\]
is canonically isomorphic to $\mathrm{Im}(\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}})$. It is the maximal quotient of $A$ detected by the family of componentwise quotient maps when the component label is retained.
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Common-character quotient]\label{thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, let
\[
X_v:=\{\chi\in \widehat A\mid \chi_*(\omega_v)=0\}
\]
for each global component section $v$, and define the common annihilating characters by
\[
X^{\mathrm{com}}(r):=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v.
\]
Then
\[
X^{\mathrm{com}}(r)=\mathrm{Ann}\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr).
\]
Consequently, the quotient
\[
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r):=A\Big/\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
\]
is exactly the quotient detected by the characters that annihilate every componentwise homological image simultaneously.
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Exact comparison of the aggregate quotients]\label{thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map}, there is a short exact sequence
\[
0\longrightarrow K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\xrightarrow{\ \beta_r\ } Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\longrightarrow 0.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\ker(\beta_r)
\cong
\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr).
\]
Thus the component-forgetting kernel is the quotient of the sum of the componentwise images by their intersection.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Criterion for equality of the aggregate quotients]\label{cor:no-branch-gain-criterion}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map}, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\beta_r$ is an isomorphism;
\item $K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)=0$;
\item
\[
\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
=
\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v;
\]
\item the homological image subgroups are independent of the component section:
\[
K_v=K_w
\qquad
(\forall v,w\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)).
\]
\end{enumerate}
Hence retaining the component label changes no quotient information exactly when all global component sections have the same presentation-relative homological image subgroup.
\end{corollary}
\begin{corollary}[Dual character description of the component-forgetting kernel]\label{cor:branch-resolution-dual}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map},
\[
\widehat{Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)}
\cong
\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v,
\qquad
\widehat{Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)}
\cong
\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v.
\]
Moreover,
\[
\widehat{K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)}
\cong
\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v\Bigr).
\]
Thus the dual of the component-forgetting kernel consists of the characters generated by the componentwise class-trivializing characters, modulo those that are common to every component.
\end{corollary}
\begin{theorem}[Exact logarithmic component-forgetting fibre cardinality]\label{thm:branch-resolution-budget}
Every auxiliary register for the component-forgetting map satisfies
\[
|R|\ge |K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)|
=
\left|\Bigl(\sum_v K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_v K_v\Bigr)\right|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2|R|
\ge
\log_2|K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)|.
\]
This bound is sharp.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Factorization of uniform quotient-fibre information]\label{cor:uniform-hidden-budget-factorization}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map},
\[
\left|\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\right|
=
|K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)|
\cdot
\left|\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\right|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2\left|\sum_v K_v\right|
=
\log_2|K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)|
+
\log_2\left|\bigcap_v K_v\right|.
\]
Thus the logarithmic fibre cardinality of the common-character quotient splits into the component-forgetting contribution and the logarithmic cardinality of the intersection of the componentwise images.
\end{corollary}
\begin{theorem}[Common-register fibre cardinality]\label{thm:multi-branch-strictification-budget}
Every common auxiliary register for the componentwise quotient maps satisfies
\[
|R|\ge \max_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} |K_v|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2|R|\ge \max_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} \log_2|K_v|.
\]
This bound is sharp.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Single-component reduction]\label{cor:single-branch-reduction}
If
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v\},
\]
then
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong Q_v
\]
and the minimum common auxiliary-register cardinality is exactly $|K_v|$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Abramsky--Brandenburger support interface]\label{prop:ab-compatibility-translation}
For every finite empirical model \(e\), the following sets are naturally in
bijection:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item compatible families \((s_C)_{C\in\mathcal M}\) with
      \(s_C\in S_e(C)\);
\item global assignments \(s\in\prod_{x\in X}O_x\) such that
      \(s|_C\in S_e(C)\) for every \(C\in\mathcal M\);
\item global sections of the support presheaf in the standard
      Abramsky--Brandenburger sense;
\item elements of \(\widehat S_e(X)\), equivalently elements of
      \((aS_e)(X)\) on the terminally augmented context site.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, if both \(\CompSec_s(r)\) and \(\Sec_s(r)\) are interpreted
from this same support presheaf on the standard measurement cover, then
\[
M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash\Sec_s(r).
\]
In particular, strong contextuality is absence of a compatible family at the
set-valued level; it is not the matching-without-amalgamation case
\(\CompSec_s(r)\land\neg\Sec_s(r)\) of this paper.
\end{proposition}
\begin{theorem}[Canonical split stack and the degree obstruction]\label{thm:empirical-model-stack-interface}
Let \(e\) be a finite empirical model on the context site above.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item There is a canonical banded stack lift with zero band,
\[
\mathfrak E_e^0:=\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e),
\qquad
\pi_0(\mathfrak E_e^0)\cong aS_e.
\]
Here \(\operatorname{Disc}\) regards a sheaf of sets as a stack of discrete
groupoids.
\item More generally, after an abelian sheaf \(\Band\) has been supplied,
there is a canonical split \(\Band\)-banded stack lift
\[
\mathfrak E_e^{\mathrm{spl}}:=
\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e)\times\mathsf B\Band.
\]
Every component gerbe of this lift is neutral, and therefore every
Giraud class supplied by
\Cref{thm:intrinsic-branch-obstruction-extension} is zero.
\item The assignments in \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} are pseudonatural
under context refinements: there are coherent banded equivalences
\[
u_\rho^{-1}\mathfrak E_e^0\simeq
\mathfrak E_{\rho^*e}^0,
\qquad
u_\rho^{-1}\mathfrak E_e^{\mathrm{spl}}\simeq
\mathfrak E_{\rho^*e}^{\mathrm{spl}}
\]
in the second formula whenever the supplied bands are identified under
pullback.
\item The AB and AMB constructions do not produce the non-split degree-two
data required by this paper. More precisely, fix
\(C_0\in\mathcal M\) and \(s\in S_e(C_0)\).
Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa form the free-abelian presheaf
\(\mathcal F_e=\mathbb Z[S_e]\), its relative kernel
\(\mathcal F_{e,\overline C_0}\), and a class
\[
\gamma_e(s)\in
\check H^1(\mathcal M,\mathcal F_{e,\overline C_0}).
\]
This is relative \v{C}ech cohomology of a presheaf, and the class is the
isomorphism class of an
\(\mathcal F_{e,\overline C_0}\)-torsor trivialized by
\(\mathcal M\). It is not a derived \(H^2\) Giraud class for a sheaf band
\cite{AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012,Caru2017}.
\item There is no comparison which recovers these AMB classes, for all finite
empirical models, from the component-section obstruction map of any banded stack lift
whose component sheaf is \(aS_e\). Indeed, if \(e\) is strongly contextual,
then
\[
(aS_e)(X)=\varnothing,
\]
so the component-section obstruction map
\[
\delta_{\mathfrak E_e}:(aS_e)(X)\longrightarrow
H^2(\mathcal D_{\mathcal M},\Band)
\]
has empty domain, whereas AMB classes are defined for the local sections
\(s\in S_e(C_0)\) and can be nonzero, as in their GHZ and
Kochen--Specker examples.
\end{enumerate}
In \textup{(i)}, \emph{canonical} means that the construction is the
composite of the universal sheafification functor and the discrete-stack
functor, with no choices, and that the displayed refinement equivalences
satisfy the usual pseudofunctor coherence. It does not mean that all
banded stack lifts of \(S_e\) are equivalent.
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Exact limit of transfer to empirical models]\label{thm:unique-branch-contextuality-comparison}
For finite Abramsky--Brandenburger empirical models, the set-valued
local-to-global statements of \Cref{prop:ab-compatibility-translation}
transfer functorially. The later banded and homological theorems transfer
only after a band and a non-split banded stack lift have been
supplied as additional data. In particular:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the canonical split lift gives only zero Giraud classes and zero
homological image subgroups;
\item the characterization
\[
d(G)\le 2\beta
\quad\text{and}\quad
G\text{ is not a cyclic }p\text{-group}
\]
remains a construction theorem for the chosen wedge-of-spheres good-cover, band, and
two-component cocycle data of
\Cref{thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}; it is not a characterization of
groups canonically attached to empirical models;
\item without an additional degree-shifting construction, the Giraud
\(H^2\)-class neither recovers nor refines the AMB relative
\(\check H^1\)-obstruction.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Cocycle-coordinate quotient]\label{thm:initial-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, let
\[
\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}
\]
be the canonical quotient map. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the pushed-forward cocycle $\pi_*(\alpha)$ is pointwise zero on every nonempty triple overlap;
\item if $\varphi:A\to B$ is any homomorphism into an abelian group $B$ such that $\varphi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ for every ordered nondegenerate triple overlap, then there exists a unique homomorphism
\[
\bar\varphi:Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}\to B
\]
with $\varphi=\bar\varphi\circ\pi$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence \(Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}\) is the elementary quotient of \(A\) by the
subgroup generated by the displayed cocycle values; equivalently, it is the
initial coefficient quotient through which this chosen obstruction
cocycle representative becomes pointwise zero.
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Characters of the cocycle-coordinate quotient]\label{thm:character-definability-barrier}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, let
\[
\widehat A:=\mathrm{Hom}(A,\mathbb T),
\]
where $\mathbb T\subseteq\mathbb C^\times$ is the unit circle, and define
\[
H_\alpha^\perp:=\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi(h)=1\text{ for every }h\in H_\alpha\}.
\]
Call a character $\chi\in\widehat A$ \emph{cocycle-trivializing} for the chosen representative if
\[
\chi(\alpha_{ijk})=1
\]
for every nonempty triple overlap of $\mathcal U$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a character $\chi\in\widehat A$ is cocycle-trivializing if and only if $\chi\in H_\alpha^\perp$;
\item the quotient map $\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}$ induces a natural isomorphism
\[
\widehat{Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}}\cong H_\alpha^\perp;
\]
\item consequently, every cocycle-trivializing character factors uniquely through $Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}$, and a character annihilates the coordinate-image subgroup if and only if it lies in $H_\alpha^\perp$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Detection by annihilating characters]\label{cor:visible-quotient-character-separation}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, for elements $x,y\in A$ the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item $\pi(x)=\pi(y)$ in $Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}$;
\item $\chi(x)=\chi(y)$ for every cocycle-trivializing character $\chi\in\widehat A$.
\end{enumerate}
Equivalently, $Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}$ is the quotient of $A$ obtained by identifying exactly those elements that cannot be separated by cocycle-trivializing characters.
\end{corollary}
\begin{theorem}[Fibre size of an auxiliary register]\label{thm:minimal-information-cost-of-strictification}
In the setting of \Cref{def:auxiliary-strictification-code}, every fibre-separating auxiliary register satisfies
\[
|R|\ge |H_\alpha|.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\log_2 |R|\ge \log_2 |H_\alpha|.
\]
Moreover, this lower bound is sharp.
\leanverified{paper_gluing_failure_minimal_information_cost_of_strictification}
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Minimum auxiliary register size]\label{cor:conservative-recovery-budget}
For a finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative, an injective lift that leaves the quotient coordinate unchanged requires at least \(\log_2|H_\alpha|\) bits in its auxiliary register. This is the elementary fibre-counting consequence of quotienting by the coordinate-image subgroup \(H_\alpha\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Typed-expansion construction with neutral and non-neutral component gerbes on a finite intersection site]\label{thm:mixed-component-construction}
Let
\[
\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
be a finite good-cover presentation in a pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$ whose nonempty \v{C}ech nerve
\[
N:=N(\mathcal U)
\]
is connected. Equivalently, the geometric realization of the cover nerve is a
paracompact space homotopy equivalent to $|N|$ and all nonempty finite
intersections of members of $\mathcal U$ are acyclic for constant
coefficients. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}; it contains the strict
initial object $\emptyset$ for empty intersections, and the \v{C}ech
cochain complex below omits those pullback objects only because
$A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0$. Let $A$ be a finite abelian group, and let
$A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine constant sheaf on the finite
intersection site $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ whose value on each nonempty
basic intersection is identified with $A$. View $A_{\mathcal U}$ as the
automorphism band of the banded groupoid lift constructed below, and
assume
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Use the Leray comparison isomorphism of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv). The typed-expansion
construction below is relative to the deliberately broad
typed-expansion semantics of
\artresult{Typed-expansion model-class interface for the layer chain}. Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, and let
\[
\phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
be a homomorphism for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$. Define
\[
\Lambda_0:=\{\lambda\in\Lambda\mid \phi_\lambda=0\}.
\]
Then, in the typed-expansion model class generated by
\artresult{Typed-expansion model-class interface for the layer chain}, the following typed data
define a one-state $\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3$ model \(M\): the one-state
\(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the finite intersection site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), the
presheaf of local sections \(F_{p,s}\) constructed below, and the banded groupoid
lift
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
\]
with band \(A_{\mathcal U}\). In that generated model there are a state
\(p\) and a distinguished admissible parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) with
\([r]_p=a\), local slice site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and
\(\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)\), such that the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda.
\]
\item Under the identification in \textup{(i)}, for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$ the corresponding component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ has component obstruction class
\[
\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
\]
satisfying
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
\qquad
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
Consequently,
\[
\mathfrak G_\lambda\ \text{is neutral}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\phi_\lambda=0.
\]
\item $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$. More precisely, if $\Lambda_0=\varnothing$ this is the vacuous terminal-fibre case
\[
\mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing=\mathfrak L_r(a),
\]
while if $\Lambda_0\neq\varnothing$ the terminal objects of $\mathfrak L_r(a)$ lie exactly in the neutral components indexed by $\Lambda_0$ and are in the essential image of $\mathfrak P_r(a)$. In either case
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Lambda_0\neq\varnothing.
\]
\item
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r),
\]
and
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Lambda_0=\varnothing.
\]
\item The aggregate quotients satisfy
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Exact subgroup classification on torsion-free second homology]\label{cor:torsion-free-h2-classification}
Assume the full hypotheses of \Cref{thm:mixed-component-construction}: a
fixed finite nerve $N=N(\mathcal U)$ in the finite intersection site associated to a good-cover nerve,
a trivialized constant finite abelian band $A$, the Leray comparison
isomorphism for the finite-nerve classes, the constructed banded groupoid
lift, and essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre. Assume
in addition that
\[
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong \mathbb Z^\beta
\]
for some integer $\beta\ge 0$. Let $(K_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\Lambda}$ be a family of subgroups of $A$. Then the following are equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item There exist a state $p$, an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ on the fixed nerve $N$, and a bijection
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda
\]
such that the component indexed by $\lambda$ has presentation-relative homological image subgroup
\[
K_\lambda.
\]
\item Every subgroup $K_\lambda$ is generated by at most $\beta$ elements.
\end{enumerate}
When these conditions hold,
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}K_\lambda,
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}K_\lambda,
\]
and
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
K_\lambda=0\ \text{for some}\ \lambda\in\Lambda.
\]
In particular, a single component attains surjective homological evaluation
\[
K_\omega=A
\]
if and only if $A$ is generated by at most $\beta$ elements. For $\beta=1$, the attainable single-component presentation-relative homological image subgroups are exactly the cyclic subgroups of $A$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{lemma}[$2$-cocycle-defined banded prestack for one component]\label{lem:twisted-branch-prestack-model}
Let $\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}$ be a finite cover whose nerve $N$ is connected, let $A$ be a finite abelian group, let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty label set, and let
\[
\alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)
\qquad
(\lambda\in\Lambda)
\]
be normalized simplicial cocycles. Assume also that
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
We use their alternating extension to all
ordered triples of indices: for pairwise distinct indices, permuting an
ordered simplex multiplies the value by the sign of the permutation, and any
triple with a repeated index has value $0$. Let
\[
A_{\mathcal U}
\]
denote the genuine constant sheaf on $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, i.e. the
sheaf of locally constant $A$-valued functions on the chosen good-cover
geometric realization restricted to the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve. Hence
$A_{\mathcal U}(U_J)=A$ for each nonempty basic intersection and
$A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0$.
Let
\[
\Lambda_0:=\{\lambda\in\Lambda\mid \alpha_\lambda=0\}.
\]
On the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, define $F$ by
\[
F(W)=
\begin{cases}
\Lambda,& W\to a\text{ a nonempty proper formal intersection object},\\
\Lambda_0,& W=a,\\
\{\ast\},& W=\emptyset,
\end{cases}
\]
with identity restrictions among nonempty proper intersection objects,
inclusions $\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda$ from $a$ to nonempty proper
intersections, and the unique restriction map to $F(\emptyset)$. For each
$\lambda\notin\Lambda_0$, define a fibred groupoid $\mathfrak P_\lambda$ by
\[
\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\varnothing
\]
and, for every nonempty proper object $W$ of $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$, put
\[
I(W):=\{i\in I\mid W\to U_i\text{ in }\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\}.
\]
Thus $I(U_J)=J$ for a formal intersection object. Define objects
\[
x_i^{(\lambda,W)}\qquad (i\in I(W))
\]
and morphism sets
\[
\mathrm{Hom}\bigl(x_i^{(\lambda,W)},x_j^{(\lambda,W)}\bigr)
=A_{\mathcal U}(W),
\]
which is identified with \(A\) because \(W\) is a connected nonempty
basic intersection. With this identification, write morphisms as
\((a,i,j)\) with \(a\in A\), and define composition by
\[
(b,j,k)\circ(a,i,j)=(a+b+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,k).
\]
For a morphism $V\to W$, one has $I(W)\subseteq I(V)$, because a map
$W\to U_i$ composes with $V\to W$. Restrictions therefore send
$x_i^{(\lambda,W)}$ to $x_i^{(\lambda,V)}$ and $(a,i,j)$ to the same
coefficient-labelled morphism after applying the restriction map of
\(A_{\mathcal U}\). Over $\emptyset$ take the terminal groupoid and let
every restriction to $\emptyset$ be the unique functor. For each
$\lambda\in\Lambda_0$, let $\mathfrak P_\lambda$ be the neutral stack with
one object over every object and automorphism group
\(A_{\mathcal U}(W)\) over \(W\), so the fibre over \(\emptyset\) is
terminal because \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\). Restrictions are exactly
the restriction maps of the sheaf \(A_{\mathcal U}\). Its stack property is
the \(H^1\)-vanishing criterion of
\Cref{lem:formal-site-neutral-stack-h1}: object descent data are
\(A_{\mathcal U}\)-torsor cocycles on slices, and those torsors are
trivial on the formal good-cover slices used here. Its automorphism sheaf is
therefore the genuine constant sheaf $A_{\mathcal U}$. Set
\[
\mathfrak P:=\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathfrak P_\lambda.
\]
This denotes the fibrewise coproduct over every nonempty object, while over
$\emptyset$ the summands share the single terminal fibre and all
restrictions to $\emptyset$ are the unique functor. The band used below is
the genuine constant sheaf $A_{\mathcal U}$ on the finite intersection site, not the
identity-restriction constant presheaf.
Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item each nonzero summand $\mathfrak P_\lambda$ is a category fibred in groupoids, and the pullback functors are strictly functorial;
\item the identity, inverse, and associativity laws in each twisted fibre are respectively the normalized identities
\[
\alpha_{\lambda,iij}=\alpha_{\lambda,ijj}=0,
\qquad
\alpha_{\lambda,iji}=0,
\]
and the cocycle identity
\[
\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
+\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}=0;
\]
\item $\mathfrak P$ is a prestack with genuine constant band $A_{\mathcal U}$;
\item its presheaf of connected components is $F$;
\item after stackification, the components over $a$ are naturally indexed by $\Lambda$, and the component indexed by $\lambda$ has \v{C}ech cocycle $\alpha_\lambda$ on $\mathcal U$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{theorem}[Construction with prescribed componentwise homological image subgroups]\label{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}
Let
\[
\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
\]
be a finite good-cover presentation in a pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$ whose nonempty \v{C}ech nerve
\[
N:=N(\mathcal U)
\]
is connected. Equivalently, the geometric realization of the cover nerve is a
paracompact space homotopy equivalent to $|N|$ and all nonempty finite
intersections of members of $\mathcal U$ are acyclic for constant
coefficients. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}; it includes the strict
initial object $\emptyset$ for all empty intersections, so disjoint cover
members have a pullback in the site even though they do not contribute a
nonzero finite-nerve cochain. Let $A$ be a finite abelian group, and let
$A_{\mathcal U}$ denote the genuine constant sheaf on
$\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}$ whose value on each nonempty basic intersection
is identified with $A$. View $A_{\mathcal U}$ as the automorphism band of
the banded groupoid lift constructed below, and assume
\[
H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Use the Leray comparison isomorphism of
\Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}(iv). Let $\Lambda$ be a finite nonempty set, and let
\[
\phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
be a nonzero homomorphism for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$. Then, in the
typed-expansion model class generated by
\artresult{Typed-expansion model-class interface for the layer chain}, the following typed data
define a one-state $\mathbb L_2/\mathbb L_3$ model \(M\): the one-state
\(\mathbb L_1\)-reduct, the finite intersection site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), the
presheaf of local sections \(F_{p,s}\) constructed below, and the banded groupoid lift
prestack
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_{\mathcal U}
\]
with band \(A_{\mathcal U}\). In that generated model there are a state
\(p\) and a distinguished admissible parameter \(r:\mathsf{Ref}_s\) with
\([r]_p=a\), local slice site \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\), and
\(\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)\), such that the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item
\[
\Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \Lambda;
\]
\item under the identification in \textup{(i)}, for each $\lambda\in\Lambda$ the corresponding component gerbe $\mathfrak G_\lambda\subseteq \mathfrak L_r$ has component obstruction class
\[
\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)
\]
satisfying
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
\qquad
K_\lambda=\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda);
\]
\item $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$ by the vacuous terminal-fibre case
\[
\mathfrak P_r(a)=\varnothing=\mathfrak L_r(a),
\]
and
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r);
\]
\item the aggregate quotients satisfy
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\cong A\Big/\bigcap_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
\qquad
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\cong A\Big/\sum_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda).
\]
\end{enumerate}
In particular, within the fixed finite-nerve and typed-expansion
framework above, every finite family of subgroups of $A$ arising as images of homomorphisms
\[
H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A
\]
is attained as the family of presentation-relative homological image subgroups of the global component sections of one admissible parameter.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Global sections yield compatible local sections]\label{prop:global-implies-compatible-local}
In the pullback-based site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, so without assuming a meet-semilattice presentation of overlaps, if $M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)$, then
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
M,p\Vdash \LocSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{lemma}[Explicit plus--plus normal form for sheafification]\label{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let
$F$ be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let
$u:F\to aF$ be the sheafification unit. For every object $X$ and every
section $\xi\in(aF)(X)$ there is a covering family
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
and sections $\sigma_i\in F(X_i)$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item for every pair $(i,j)$, the restrictions of $\sigma_i$ and
$\sigma_j$ to the pullback $X_i\times_X X_j$ agree after a covering
refinement of that pullback object;
\item the family $(u(\sigma_i))_i$ glues in the sheaf $aF$ to $\xi$;
\end{enumerate}
No strict matching family of $F$ is asserted in this first conclusion.
For an arbitrary presheaf, the compatibility obtained from a section of
\((aF)(X)\) is only compatibility after refinements of the pairwise
overlaps. This is exactly the loss of information caused by the two
successive plus-construction quotient relations. In particular, the data
in \textup{(i)} cannot be used as cover-level identity descent data unless
the refined equalities have first been upgraded to equalities on the
original pullback overlaps.

If $F$ is separated at every object of the slice $\mathcal C/X$, then the
equalities after covering refinements in \textup{(i)} upgrade to
equalities in $F(X_i\times_X X_j)$; in that case the displayed data are a
strict matching family in $F$ on the cover of $X$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Compatible representatives of a sheafified section]\label{lem:strictify-component-representatives}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let
$F$ be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let \(X\) be an object.
Assume that \(F\) is separated at every object of the slice
\(\mathcal C/X\). If \(\xi\in(aF)(X)\), then there is a cover
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
and sections \(\sigma_i\in F(X_i)\) which form a strict matching family on
the original pullback overlaps \(X_i\times_X X_j\), and whose images under
the sheafification unit glue to \(\xi\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Descent of refined equality for a separated restriction]\label{lem:separated-restriction-refined-equality}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let $F$
be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let $X$ be an object. Suppose
that $F$ is separated at every object of the slice $\mathcal C/X$. If
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
is a cover and sections $\sigma_i\in F(X_i)$ satisfy the following
refined-overlap condition: for every pair $(i,j)$, writing
$X_{ij}:=X_i\times_X X_j$, the two restrictions of $\sigma_i$ and
$\sigma_j$ to $X_{ij}$ become equal after some covering family of
$X_{ij}$, then the family $(\sigma_i)_i$ is a strict matching family:
\[
\res_{X_{ij}}^{X_i}(\sigma_i)=\res_{X_{ij}}^{X_j}(\sigma_j)
\qquad(\forall i,j).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Equality in the unit is local equality]\label{lem:sheafification-unit-equality-local}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let
$F$ be a presheaf of sets on $\mathcal C$, and let
$u:F\to aF$ be the sheafification unit. If
\[
u_X(\sigma)=u_X(\tau)
\qquad
(\sigma,\tau\in F(X)),
\]
then there is a covering family $\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}$ such that
\[
\res_{X_i}^{X}(\sigma)=\res_{X_i}^{X}(\tau)
\qquad(\forall i\in I).
\]
Conversely, equality after such a cover implies
$u_X(\sigma)=u_X(\tau)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proposition}[Existence of a matching family gives a sheafified section]\label{prop:compsec-to-sheafification}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at $p$, and write $a=[r]_p$. Then, in the pullback-based thin site $\mathcal C_{p,s}$ of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame},
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{theorem}[Sheafification characterizes existence of a matching family on slice sites on which the restricted presheaf is separated]\label{thm:sheafification-characterization}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at $p$, write $a=[r]_p$, and assume that
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice in the sense of
\Cref{def:separated-restriction}. Then, in the pullback-based thin
site $\mathcal C_{p,s}$ of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame},
\[
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Sheaf condition at a reference]\label{cor:sheaf-local-to-global}
In the pullback-based thin site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, assume
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice. If $F_{p,s}$ is already a
sheaf at $a=[r]_p$, then existence of a global section and existence of a matching family coincide:
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{corollary}[Sheafification removes matching without amalgamation]\label{cor:sheafification-removes-glue}
Let $\Sec_s^{\sharp}(r)$ denote existence of a global section computed with the
sheafified presheaf of local sections $a_{p,s}F_{p,s}$. In the pullback-based
thin site of \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}, assume that
$F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice. Then
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s^{\sharp}(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r)
\]
In particular, no admissible parameter remains in matching without amalgamation after sheafification.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[Separated split banded groupoid lift]\label{prop:split-banded-groupoid-lift}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,\qquad \mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let $\Band$ be an abelian sheaf on $\mathcal C_a$. There is a canonical split banded groupoid lift
\[
\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}\to\mathcal C_a
\]
with band $\Band$ and connected-component presheaf
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}})
\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\]
Explicitly, for every object $U$ of $\mathcal C_a$, the groupoid
$\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}(U)$ has object set $F_{p,s}(U)$ and
\[
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak P_r^{\mathrm{spl}}(U)}(\sigma,\tau)=
\begin{cases}
\Band(U),& \sigma=\tau,\\
\varnothing,& \sigma\ne\tau,
\end{cases}
\]
with composition given by addition in $\Band(U)$. For a morphism
$f:V\to U$ in $\mathcal C_a$, restriction sends $\sigma$ to
$\res_V^U(\sigma)$ and sends an automorphism $b\in\Band(U)$ to its
restriction $b|_V\in\Band(V)$.

This split prestack is separated as a prestack in groupoids, in the
sense that every isomorphism presheaf is a sheaf, if and only if the
restricted presheaf of local sections $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is
separated on the slice in the sense of \Cref{def:separated-restriction}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Consequences of a chosen gluing-sensitive banded lift datum]\label{prop:gluing-sensitive-lift-consequences}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, and suppose $r$ is equipped with a gluing-sensitive banded lift datum in the sense of \Cref{def:gluing-sensitive-banded-lift}. Then the later component-obstruction theory depends on this extra datum, rather than on the split prestack of \Cref{prop:split-banded-groupoid-lift}, as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the sheafified global sections of $r$ are identified with $\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)$ via \Cref{thm:visible-value-components};
\item if the cofinal gerbe-adapted-cover hypothesis of
\artresult{Component gerbes and component obstruction classes} is added,
then each global component section determines a component gerbe as in
\artresult{Supporting component gerbes of global component sections};
\item if, in addition, the stackification map is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre and $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated when the matching-family predicate \(\CompSec_s\) is invoked, then the comparison between global sections and neutral component gerbes is exactly the one proved in
\artresult{Global sections versus neutral component gerbes} and
\artresult{Component-gerbe criterion for matching without amalgamation}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Components of the stackification]\label{prop:stackification-components}
In the setting of \Cref{def:banded-groupoid-lift} over the pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$, so without assuming an ambient meet-semilattice presentation, the sheaf of connected components of $\mathfrak L_r$ is canonically identified with the sheafification of the restricted presheaf of local sections $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Naturality of the stackification component map]\label{prop:stackification-component-unit-naturality}
In the setting of \Cref{def:banded-groupoid-lift}, the map of component
sheaves induced by the stackification morphism
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to a(\mathfrak P_r)=\mathfrak L_r
\]
is, under the identification
\[
\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P_r)\cong F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\quad\text{and}\quad
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)\cong a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a},
\]
exactly the sheafification unit
\[
u:F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\longrightarrow
a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}.
\]
Equivalently, for every object \(U\) of the slice and every
\(y\in\mathfrak P_r(U)\),
\[
[\iota_r(y)]=u_U([y])
\]
in \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(U)\).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[No new global objects at $a$]\label{prop:no-new-global-objects}
Assume essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre over $a$ in the sense of \Cref{def:global-conservativity} for a banded groupoid lift on the pullback-based slice site $\mathcal C_a$. Then
\[
M,p\Vdash \Sec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak L_r(a)\neq\varnothing.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{lemma}[Where separatedness on the slice enters]\label{lem:unit-equality-on-cover-members}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let \(F\)
be a presheaf of sets, let \(u:F\to aF\) be the sheafification unit, and
let \(X\) be an object. Assume that \(F\) is separated at every object of
the slice \(\mathcal C/X\). If
\[
\{X_i\to X\}_{i\in I}
\]
is a covering family, \(\sigma\in F(X)\), and
\(\sigma_i\in F(X_i)\) are sections satisfying
\[
u_{X_i}\bigl(\res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma\bigr)=u_{X_i}(\sigma_i)
\qquad(\forall i),
\]
then
\[
\res_{X_i}^{X}\sigma=\sigma_i
\qquad(\forall i).
\]
\end{lemma}
