\begin{proposition}[Sheafified global sections as sections of the sheaf of connected components]\label{thm:visible-value-components}
Assume that $r$ admits a banded groupoid lift in the sense of \Cref{def:banded-groupoid-lift} over the pullback-based slice site attached to \Cref{def:local-reference-frame}. Then the sheafified global sections of $r$ are exactly the sections at $a$ of the sheafification of the presheaf of local sections, and equivalently the sections at $a$ of the sheaf of connected components of $\mathfrak L_r$:
\[
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)\cong \Vis_{p,s}(r)\cong \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a).
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Address before value]\label{prop:address-before-value}
If
\[
\Read_{p,s}(r)=v
\qquad\text{with}\qquad
v\notin\{\Inadm,\Indet\},
\]
then
\[
M,p\Vdash \Adm_s(r)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
M,p\Vdash \CompSec_s(r).
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{corollary}[Criterion for a determined readout]\label{cor:nonnull-readout-criterion}
\[
\Read_{p,s}(r)=v
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\{v\}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\Read_{p,s}(r)=\Indet
\]
if and only if $r$ is admissible and either $\Vis_{p,s}(r)=\varnothing$ or $|\Vis_{p,s}(r)|>1$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proposition}[Refinement stability of defined readouts]\label{prop:typed-readout-persistence}
Let $q\le p$, and suppose
\[
\Read_{p,s}(r)=v
\qquad\text{with}\qquad
v\notin\{\Inadm,\Indet\}.
\]
Then
\[
\Read_{q,s}(r)=v.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[The three cases imply failure of global existence]\label{prop:null-modes-imply-null}
For every admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$,
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoLoc_s(r)\ \lor\ \NoMatch_s(r)\ \lor\ \NoAmalg_s(r)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash \NoGlob_s(r).
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Mutual exclusivity of the three cases]\label{prop:null-modes-exclusive}
No two distinct failure cases can hold simultaneously for the same parameter at the same state.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Trichotomy for failure of global existence]\label{prop:null-trichotomy}
Assume that the local-section predicates are decided at $p$ for the sort $s$, and let
\[
r:\mathsf{Ref}_s
\]
be admissible at $p$. If
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoGlob_s(r),
\]
then exactly one of the three formulas
\[
\NoLoc_s(r),\qquad
\NoMatch_s(r),\qquad
\NoAmalg_s(r)
\]
holds at $p$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{theorem}[Classification after refinement]\label{thm:null-classification-under-refinement}
Let
\[
q\le p,
\]
assume that the local-section predicates are decided at $q$ for the sort $s$, and let
\[
r:\mathsf{Ref}_s
\]
be admissible at $q$. If
\[
M,q\Vdash \NoGlob_s(r),
\]
then exactly one of the formulas
\[
\NoLoc_s(r),\qquad
\NoMatch_s(r),\qquad
\NoAmalg_s(r)
\]
holds at $q$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Parameter-free one-reference lower-fragment separation in the constant-free reduct]\label{thm:pointwise-irreducibility}
There exist a model $M$, the singleton information state
\[
p=(\varnothing,\{\emptyset\}),
\]
and pairwise distinct elements
\[
a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4\in D_{\mathsf{Ref}_s}^M
\]
of one parameter sort such that:
The comparison language in this theorem is restricted to the
parameter-free one-variable \(\Sigma_1\)-reduct: it excludes admission,
all local-section predicates, site predicates, cover data, and parameters of
that parameter sort. The undefinability conclusions are therefore conclusions on
the admissible subdomain displayed in \textup{(i)}, not definitions of
admission itself in the lower language.
No separation claim is made for languages with such parameters or with
access to the \(\Sigma_2\) presheaf structure.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item each $a_i$ is admissible at $p$, and for every $\varphi(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\mathrm{inv}}$,
\[
M,\emptyset[x\mapsto a_i]\models\varphi(x)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,\emptyset[x\mapsto a_j]\models\varphi(x)
\]
for all $i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\}$;
\item
\[
M,p\Vdash \bigl(\CompSec_s(x)\land \Sec_s(x)\bigr)[a_1/x];
\]
\item
\[
M,p\Vdash \bigl(\CompSec_s(x)\land \neg\Sec_s(x)\bigr)[a_2/x];
\]
\item
\[
M,p\Vdash \bigl(\LocSec_s(x)\land \neg\CompSec_s(x)\bigr)[a_3/x];
\]
\item
\[
M,p\Vdash \bigl(\neg\LocSec_s(x)\bigr)[a_4/x].
\]
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, on the displayed admissible subdomain, no $\varphi(x)\in\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\mathrm{inv}}$ can define any of the predicates
\[
\LocSec_s,
\qquad
\CompSec_s,
\qquad
\Sec_s,
\qquad
\NoLoc_s,
\qquad
\NoMatch_s,
\qquad
\NoAmalg_s
\]
on admissible parameter elements. In particular, $\CompSec_s$, $\Sec_s$, and $\NoAmalg_s$ are not reducible to the automorphism-invariant pointwise fragment of the $\mathbb L_1$-reduct.
\end{theorem}

 succeeded in 185ms:
\begin{lemma}[Bijective terminal unit and the terminal sheaf axiom]\label{lem:bijective-unit-terminal-sheaf-axiom}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a site with pullbacks of covering families, let $F$
be a presheaf of sets, let $X$ be an object, and let
\[
u:F\longrightarrow aF
\]
be the sheafification unit. Assume that \(F\) is separated at every object
of the slice \(\mathcal C/X\). Then the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
\item the map \(u_X:F(X)\to(aF)(X)\) is bijective;
\item \(F\) satisfies the sheaf axiom at \(X\): every strict matching
family on every cover of \(X\) has a unique amalgamation in \(F(X)\).
\end{enumerate}
Here separatedness on the whole slice is essential.  In the direction from
bijectivity of \(u_X\) to the sheaf axiom, the chosen preimage of the
glued sheaf section has restrictions which agree with the prescribed
local sections only after applying \(u\) over each cover member.  Equality
in \((aF)(X_i)\) gives equality in \(F\) only after a further cover of
\(X_i\), and separatedness at \(X_i\), not merely at \(X\), is what
descends that refined equality back to \(X_i\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Isomorphism torsor for a lifted component]\label{lem:canonical-lift-isom-torsor}
Let $\mathfrak P_r$ be a representative-rigid banded groupoid lift with band $\Band$ on $\mathcal C_a$, let
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)
\]
be the stackification map, and let $x\in\mathfrak L_r(a)$ and $\sigma\in F_{p,s}(a)$ satisfy
\[
[\iota_r(s_a(\sigma))]=[x]
\quad\text{in}\quad
\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a).
\]
Then the sheaf
\[
\mathcal I_{\sigma,x}:=
\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L_r}(\iota_r(s_a(\sigma)),x)
\]
is a $\Band$-torsor on $\mathcal C_a$. If $H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)=0$, then $\mathcal I_{\sigma,x}$ has a global section, equivalently
\[
\iota_r(s_a(\sigma))\cong x
\quad\text{in}\quad
\mathfrak L_r(a).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{lemma}[Representing component sections by descent]\label{lem:component-section-descent-object}
Let $\mathfrak P_r$ be a representative-rigid banded groupoid lift on
$\mathcal C_a$, assume that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is
separated on the slice, and let
\[
\iota_r:\mathfrak P_r\to\mathfrak L_r:=a(\mathfrak P_r)
\]
be the stackification map. Every section
\[
\xi\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L_r)(a)
\]
is represented by an object of the terminal stack fibre $\mathfrak L_r(a)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{theorem}[Rigidity of representative-rigid lifts over slice sites on which the restricted presheaf is separated]\label{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
and let
\[
\mathfrak P_r\to \mathcal C_a
\]
be a representative-rigid banded groupoid lift with band $\Band$. Let
\[
u:F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}\longrightarrow a_{p,s}F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\]
be the sheafification unit. Assume throughout this theorem that
\[
F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}
\]
is separated on the slice in the sense of
\Cref{def:separated-restriction}; equivalently, $F_{p,s}$ is
separated at every object of the slice $\mathcal C_a$, including every
cover member, every pullback overlap, and every refinement occurring below.
Separatedness only at the terminal object $a$ is not sufficient for this
theorem, and no assertion below is made under that weaker hypothesis.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item If $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$, then
\[
u_a:F_{p,s}(a)\to \bigl(a_{p,s}F_{p,s}\bigr)(a)
\]
is surjective.
\item If
\[
H^1(\mathcal C_a,\Band)=0,
\]
then surjectivity of $u_a$ implies that $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially
surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$.
\end{enumerate}
Under the same vanishing hypothesis the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
\item $\mathfrak P_r$ is essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$;
\item $u_a$ is bijective;
\item $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ satisfies the sheaf gluing condition at
the object $a$, that is, every matching family on every cover of $a$ has
an amalgamation in $F_{p,s}(a)$; uniqueness follows from the standing
separatedness on the slice at $a$.
\end{enumerate}
The last equivalence is not a consequence of separatedness at \(a\) alone.
It uses separatedness at all cover members, pullback overlaps, and their
refinements in the slice \(\mathcal C_a\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{corollary}[Matching without amalgamation requires noncanonical lift data]\label{cor:no-rigid-gluing-null}
Let $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ be admissible at a state $p$, write
\[
a=[r]_p,
\qquad
\mathcal C_a:=\mathcal C_{p,s}/a,
\]
assume that $F_{p,s}|_{\mathcal C_a}$ is separated on the slice. If
\[
M,p\Vdash \NoAmalg_s(r),
\]
then no representative-rigid banded groupoid lift with band $\Band$ can be essentially surjective on the terminal fibre over $a$.
\end{corollary}

# Theorem Inventory

This Stage A inventory was prepared against `scope_contract.md`, `scope_contract.json`, `research_directive.md`, the compiled manuscript sources included by `main.tex`, all `.tex` files in the paper directory, and `references.bib`.

## Summary

The compiled manuscript contains the A/B/C theorem chain: extension-chain forcing, pullback-based local sites and sheafification, terminal-fibre rigidity, lower-fragment separation, component gerbes and gerbe-null semantics, strict and intrinsic visible quotients, pure Ext character blindness, branch-sensitive versus branch-uniform comparison, finite-nerve realization, the torsion-free H2 subgroup classification, bouquet two-branch resolution, the exact obstruction to transferring these results to empirical models, and the split GHZ/Mermin lift.

No required in-scope theorem from the scope contract is completely absent from the compiled manuscript. The main risks are not missing labels but proof-interface sharpness and source hygiene:

- `lem:bouquet-cover-classical-interface` uses `beta` without quantifying it.
- `lem:generated-subsite-model-interface` needs an explicit admissible-model/free-expansion convention.
- `prop:invariance-visible-quotients` must remain groupoid-indexed, not path-independent.
- `sec_forcing_necessity.tex` is an unused stronger draft with duplicate `thm:pointwise-irreducibility` and unresolved references.
- `sequel/` files are out-of-scope split material.
- Several included section files exceed the 800-line discipline.

## Files Written

- `theorem_inventory.json`
- `theorem_inventory.md`

## JSON Inventory

The authoritative machine-readable inventory is in `theorem_inventory.json`. It uses exactly the required top-level keys:

## Classification Notes

The inventory treats the compiled `main.tex` inputs as the current manuscript. It also scans unused `.tex` files because the Stage A prompt requested all TeX sources. Results in `sequel/` and `sec_forcing_necessity.tex` are therefore classified, but not as part of the compiled article.

Classical material is classified as supporting/background when it is a standard sheaf, stack, gerbe, Cech, UCT, finite abelian duality, or finite group structure fact. The paper-specific contribution is the way these tools are assembled into the state-forcing gluing-obstruction and homological-visibility chain.

The inventory records cross-resolution risks only where a future edit might accidentally convert a stated comparison or monotonicity result into a naive invariant. The current manuscript usually handles this correctly by requiring homology-equivalent refinement, chosen zigzags, Leray/good-cover comparison, or explicit pullback hypotheses.


 succeeded in 178ms:
Target journal: APAL (Annals of Pure and Applied Logic)
Previously: NDJFL (changed after internal review)

Revision summary (2026-03-23):

1. Fixed correctness issue in Theorem 4.26 (component gerbe decomposition):
   The original version claimed the full stack decomposition unconditionally.
   The proof only showed each visible branch gives a gerbe, not that every
   local branch comes from a global one. Fix: the gerbe property is now the
   theorem; the full decomposition is a corollary under an explicit branch
   constancy hypothesis.

2. Fixed correctness issue in Theorem 4.28 (gerbe-null-semantics):
   The equivalence Sec_s(r) <=> L_r(a) nonempty used the direction from
   L_r(a) to Sec_s(r) without justification. Fix: added Definition
   (global conservativity at the terminal fibre) requiring essential
   surjectivity of the stackification morphism on the terminal fibre,
   and Proposition (no new global objects at a) deriving the equivalence.

3. Added forcing necessity theorem (pointwise irreducibility of gluing
   predicates): Two admitted references are constructed that are
   indistinguishable at L_1 but one has CompSec /\ Sec while the other
   has CompSec /\ not Sec. This proves that the local-object enrichment
   is not merely a convenience but introduces genuinely new predicates.

4. Corrected the connection to sheaf-theoretic contextuality: finite support
   presheaves have canonical refinement-functorial split stacks, but their
   Giraud classes vanish. The AMB obstruction is a relative H1 torsor class
   indexed by a local section, so it is not recovered or refined by the
   manuscript's H2 branch map without additional degree-shifting data.

5. Removed all references to "larger architecture", "broader research
   program", "eleven-layer architecture". The paper is now presented as
   a self-contained four-layer fragment.

6. Moved finite-state complexity upper bounds from main text to appendix.

7. Split sec_null_decomposition.tex (1689 lines) into three files under
   800 lines each.

8. Updated abstract, introduction, preliminaries, and conclusion to
   reflect all changes.

Residual limitations:
1. The paper gives upper bounds for the support problems but not matching
   lower bounds. This is now stated as an open problem.
2. The realization layer remains abstract.

## Theorem Inventory

### Core results (7)

| # | Label | One-line statement |
|---|-------|--------------------|
| A1 | `thm:sheafification-characterization` | Compatible local sectionability at an admitted reference is equivalent to nonemptiness of the sheafified local object functor. |
| A2 | `thm:pointwise-irreducibility` | Compatible local sectionability and gluing-level absence are not definable in the information-state forcing language without the local-object enrichment. |
| B1 | `thm:component-gerbe-decomposition` | Each visible value class of an admitted reference determines a banded component gerbe over the slice site. |
| B2 | `thm:gerbe-null-semantics` | Gluing-level absence holds exactly when visible branches exist but every component gerbe is non-neutral; under global conservativity, global sectionability is equivalent to some component gerbe being neutral. |
| C1 | `thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient` | The class-admissible characters of a branch obstruction class are exactly the annihilator of the image of the homological evaluation map; the intrinsic visible quotient is the cokernel of that map. |
| C2 | `thm:character-blind-obstructions` | A branch obstruction is character-blind iff the class is a pure Ext-type residual. |
| C3 | `thm:empirical-model-stack-interface` | Every finite empirical model has a canonical refinement-functorial split stack, but its Giraud classes vanish; the AMB relative H1 torsor obstruction cannot be recovered by the H2 branch map on all finite models. |

## Stage Notes

### P2 Research Extension

**Research question:** Given an admitted reference carrying a finite abelian gluing obstruction, which part is semantically visible without enlarging the state space, and how much additional information is required to recover the hidden part?

**Answer:** Seven main results in three groups. A1-A2 justify the enrichment; B1-B2 supply the gerbe semantic framework; C1-C2 deliver the conditional quantitative answer, while C3 proves the exact limit of transfer to Abramsky--Brandenburger empirical models.

**Scope cuts:**
- `sec_observer_spacetime.tex`: Out of scope. No main-chain theorem depends on it.
- `sec_conservativity.tex`: Out of scope. No main theorem depends on it.
- Branch-budget theorems beyond the comparison sequence: Kept as secondary consequences, not main results.

**Gap analysis (4 gaps, all closed by P3):**
1. Bridge between strict and intrinsic visible quotients -- closed: bridge paragraph added in sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex
2. Standing hypotheses (H1)--(H4) environment for gerbe construction -- closed: labeled list added
3. Theorem-to-layer mapping in introduction -- closed: layer-boundary annotations added
4. S2 single-branch micro-example -- closed: `ex:s2-single-branch-maximal-collapse` added

- All four P2 gaps closed in this pass

请对上一轮第一候选作严格修正，并仅处理下述一个核心问题。

上一轮关于 crossed module 的起始断言在所写假设下错误。仅假设 \(G\) 对 \(K\) 的共轭作用提升到中心扩张

\[
1\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow \widehat K\xrightarrow{p}K\longrightarrow 1
\]

并不能推出复合边界 \(\partial:\widehat K\xrightarrow{p}K\hookrightarrow G\) 构成 crossed module。反例为

\[
G=K=C_2,\qquad \widehat K=C_2\times C_2,\qquad p(a,b)=a,
\]

其中 \(C_2\) 的非平凡元作用为

\[
(a,b)\longmapsto(a,b+a).
\]

该作用覆盖 \(K\) 上的平凡共轭作用并逐点固定中心核 \(\{0\}\times C_2\)，但对 \(u=(1,0)\) 与 \(v=(1,0)\) 有

\[
\partial(u)\cdot v=(1,1)\neq(1,0)=uvu^{-1},
\]

故第二 Peiffer 恒等式失败。请先给出无歧义的修正假设：或者直接假设 \((\widehat K,G,\partial)\) 已配备严格 crossed-module 作用，或者明确加入使提升作用与 \(\widehat K\) 内共轭一致的 Peiffer 相容条件。不得继续使用“作用可提升，故自动得到 crossed module”的表述。

在修正该错误后，上一轮提出的

\[
\tau([c_{\widehat K\to G}])=-\delta_1(G)
\]

仍未得到验证。Neeb--Wagemann--Wockel, “Making Lifting Obstructions Explicit”, Proc. Lond. Math. Soc. 106 (2013), 589--620, Problem 8.1(b), DOI 10.1112/plms/pds047, arXiv:1108.5853，仅提出

\[
\tau([c])=\pm\delta_1([G])
\]

作为期望关系；原文没有确定符号，也没有证明上一轮额外声称的 crossed-module 态射、带变换及商群拉回自然性。现稿只具备给定提升 gerbe 后的 Čech 二余循环与 Giraud 类机制，不具备局部连续或局部光滑群上同调的链模型、crossed-module 特征三余循环，亦不具备把平移覆盖与现稿有限好覆盖置于同一链级系统的比较同伦。因此，不能把该等式视为现稿工具的直接推论。

请不要再提出“有限带高阶 gerbe 的同调像与初始商”作为新结果。其代数内容只是现稿下列结论的次数替换：UCT 初始系数商、有限族交与和的短正合列、以及 \(H_r\cong\mathbb Z^\beta\) 时像子群的生成元分类；而 \(A\)-带状高阶 gerbe 的稳定分类由 \(H^r(-,A)\) 给出属于既有高阶 gerbe 理论。除非能解决标准高阶几何对象上有限带细化的存在性与选择无关性，否则该方向不是新的可署名定理。NWW Problem 8.2(a),(b) 同样不得作为本轮替代目标；其等式需要主丛同伦长正合列、Postnikov 不变量或 Serre 谱序列的链级比较，现稿并无此输入。

本轮唯一问题如下。设 \(G\) 为有限维 Lie 群，\(K\trianglelefteq G\) 为闭正规子群，\(Q=G/K\)，并假设 \(G\to Q\) 存在局部光滑截面；设 \(Z\) 为有限离散交换群，\(\widehat K\to K\) 为 \(G\)-等变中心扩张，且所给作用满足两条严格 Peiffer 恒等式，从而 \(\widehat K\to G\) 确为光滑 crossed module。固定并完整写明：局部光滑群三上同调的非齐次余边界号约定、crossed-module 特征三余循环的截面与提升公式、NWW 的 \(\tau\) 公式、提升 gerbe 三重重叠缺陷的复合次序及 Čech 余边界号约定。在这些固定约定下，证明或给出反例否定

\[
\tau([c_{\widehat K\to G}])=\varepsilon\,\delta_1(G)\in\check H^2(Q,\underline Z),
\qquad \varepsilon\in\{+1,-1\},
\]

并由逐项链级计算唯一确定 \(\varepsilon\)。完整证明必须同时完成以下不可省略的负担：构造同一覆盖或明确的共同细化；逐项识别两个二余循环；证明更换 \(Q\) 的局部截面、\(\widehat K\) 中的提升、邻域及覆盖时两侧产生相容的 Čech 余边界；说明有限离散 \(Z\) 在何处被使用；证明所得等式关于满足严格 Peiffer 相容性的中心扩张态射自然。若一般陈述失败，须给出满足上述全部假设的具体反例，并定位失败的精确链级项；不得以符号约定含混、双方同时消失的分裂例或仅在上同调群中“应当相同”的抽象论证替代。

所求答案必须是对 NWW Problem 8.1(b) 的实质性、可投稿推进：或者给出上述非平凡受限范畴中的完整比较定理及证明，或者给出推翻该受限陈述的严格反例。不要提供研究计划、概率评估、文献综述、次数平移的 UCT 推论或未经证明的自然性宣称。

不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

总判定
在关闭 Abramsky–Mansfield–Barbosa 的 H1 障碍与本文 H2 gerbe 障碍之间的次数、系数与索引域失配后，仍存在一条可以合理下注的具名开放问题路线：中心扩张所产生的 crossed-module 特征三类与提升 gerbe 的 Čech 二类之间的显式比较。除此之外，现有工具最稳妥的外部化方向，是把 H2-层面的同调像、初始系数商与多分量正合列提升到标准的有限带高阶 gerbe；该方向可形成一篇后续论文，但不解决高阶 gerbe 文献中的主要几何开放问题。
本文本身已经明确承认其主分类依赖预先选择的好覆盖、有限神经、常有限带及非分裂提升，而不是既有经验模型或其他标准对象的内在不变量。 其主要贡献被准确描述为对标准输入的组织、若干展示相对商、构造定理及有限群分类，而非新的一般 gerbe 分类原理。 更关键的是，高层模型类允许把满足类型条件的站点、预层和 prestack 数据直接作为扩张加入；这是一项模型类约定，而不是从既有对象出发的存在或闭包定理。
因此，以下排序采用“成功概率 × 层级影响”，其中影响以五级主观标度计。
排名候选类型成功概率潜在影响乘积1crossed-module 特征类与提升 gerbe 类的显式同一性具名开放问题45%4/51.802有限带高阶 gerbe 的同调像与初始中性化商非具名、外部自然65%2/51.303纯 Ext 提升障碍与同伦群扩张类的同一性具名开放问题18%4/50.72

1. 最强候选：crossed-module 特征类与提升 gerbe 类的显式比较
(a) 可望证明的精确定理
设
1⟶Z⟶K⟶K⟶1
为拓扑群或 Lie 群的中心扩张，K◃G 为闭正规子群，Q=G/K，并假设 G→Q 有局部连续或局部光滑截面，且 G 对 K 的共轭作用提升到 K。于是
K⟶G
构成 crossed module。
记
[cK→G​]∈Hloc3​(Q,Z)
为该 crossed module 的局部连续或局部光滑群上同调特征类，
δ1​(G)∈Hˇ2(Q,Z​)
为主 K-丛 G→Q 相对于 K→K 的提升障碍，亦即相应提升 gerbe 的 Giraud 类。令
τ:Hloc3​(Q,Z)⟶Hˇ2(Q,Z​)
为 Neeb–Wagemann–Wockel 给出的显式 Čech 化映射。
最理想的定理是：

Crossed-module–lifting-gerbe comparison theorem.
在上述假设及固定的 Čech 符号约定下，
τ([cK→G​])=−δ1​(G)
于 Hˇ2(Q,Z​) 中成立；该等式关于 crossed module 的态射、中心带的变换以及商群之间的拉回自然。若 Q 具有适当的 Leray 好覆盖，则同一等式在导出层上成立。

在 Z=A 为有限交换群、Q 有有限好覆盖的情形，本文的工具还可给出非核心但有意义的推论：
Kδ​:=Im(H2​(Q,Z)evδ​​A)
同时也是 crossed-module 特征类经 τ 后的同调像；商
A/Kδ​
是使该提升障碍仅余 UCT 的纯 Ext1(H1​(Q),−) 部分的初始系数商。若 H1​(Q,Z) 自由，则它是使相应提升 gerbe 中性的初始系数商。
(b) 具名问题、出处与开放状态
这正是 Neeb、Wagemann、Wockel 在 Making Lifting Obstructions Explicit, Proceedings of the London Mathematical Society 106 (2013), 589–620，§8，Problem 8.1(b)，accepted-version p. 36 中明确提出的等式
τ([c])=±δ1​([G]).
原文已经写出 τ 的 Čech 余循环公式，并把上述同一性作为期望结论。arXiv+1
截至 2026 年 8 月，本问题的开放状态只能以“未检得完整解决，可信度中等”表述，而不宜声称存在权威开放问题登记。一个支持性事实是：2017 年关于自由环群 string 2-group 模型的后续工作仍将 [NWW13, Problem 8.1(b)] 与一个较弱的消失结论并列引用，而没有援引或陈述上述完整等式。arXiv 本次检索亦未发现后来论文宣称在该一般性下证明了此等式。
(c) 现有方法可直接承担的部分
本文可以直接承担四个模块。
第一，提升 gerbe 一侧已经具备完整的局部对象—重叠同构—三重重叠缺陷余循环机制。标准提升 bundle gerbe 的中性恰好等价于结构群提升的存在。arXiv
第二，本文已经把 cover-level 的 Čech 二余循环、改变局部对象和比较同构所产生的 coboundary、覆盖细化及导出类之间的关系整理为可直接使用的形式。由此，δ1​(G) 一侧不需建立新的 gerbe 理论。
第三，带变换自然性与 banded equivalence 下 Giraud 类的传输已经齐备，适合处理中心扩张态射及符号约定变化。
第四，在等式建立后，UCT 评价、同调像、初始商及有限字符对偶可直接作为正式推论，而不是另造语义解释。本文现有的 component obstruction map 本来就对任意给定的带状栈提升适用，而不依赖特制的经验模型。
(d) 缺失结构与最可能失败机制
真正缺失的并不是 gerbe 理论，而是一个链级比较引理：


从 crossed module 的局部截面和提升数据写出代表 cK→G​ 的局部群三余循环；


对该三余循环施加 τ；


证明结果逐项等于提升 gerbe 的三重重叠缺陷余循环；


精确确定符号，而不是停留在 ±；


证明改变局部截面、提升及覆盖时，两侧以同一 coboundary 变化。


最可能的失败机制有三种：


Hloc3​(Q,Z) 所使用的局部连续群上同调模型与 Hˇ2(Q,Z​) 的系数层并非在全部拓扑群类别中无条件比较；


G→Q 缺乏足够良好的局部截面、数值覆盖或局部可缩性，使 τ 与 gerbe 展示不在同一 Čech 系统中；


共轭作用到 K 的提升仅弱相容，而非严格 crossed-module 数据，从而出现额外的 coherence 二余边界。


因此应首先在有限维 Lie 群、可数好覆盖、光滑中心带的范畴中证明，再判断能否推广到局部可缩拓扑群。若一开始追求全部拓扑群一般性，成功概率会显著下降。
(e) 概率与影响
成功概率：45%。
若得到完整的拓扑群或 Lie 群自然性定理，并真正解决 Problem 8.1(b)，其影响可达较强专门期刊层级；合理目标包括 Proceedings of the London Mathematical Society 以下一档、Journal of Homotopy and Related Structures、Transformation Groups 或 Advances in Geometry。若仅在有限维紧 Lie 群及好覆盖下完成，则影响下降为普通专门期刊层级。
这是三项候选中唯一兼具“具名问题”“现有 Čech–gerbe 工具直接相关”“核心缺口可压缩为一个明确比较引理”三项条件者。它应被视为首选后续项目。
研究规模：一篇后续论文可补齐。

2. 次强候选：有限带高阶 gerbe 的同调像与初始中性化商
(a) 可望证明的精确定理
最有价值的形式不应只重做 2-gerbe，而应给出次数一致的定理。
设 X 为有限 CW 复形，A 为有限交换群，r≥2，G 为一个 abelian Br−2A-gerbe，其稳定等价类
ξ=[G]∈Hr(X,A).
由 UCT 定义
evξ​:Hr​(X,Z)⟶A,Kξ​=Im(evξ​),Qξ​=A/Kξ​.
可望证明：

Higher-gerbe homological quotient theorem.


Kξ​ 与 Qξ​ 仅依赖于 G 的稳定带状等价类，并关于空间拉回与带同态自然。


对每个满射 q:A↠B，以下等价：
q 经 A↠Qξ​ 因子化;
q(Kξ​)=0;
q∗​ξ∈Ext1(Hr−1​(X,Z),B)⊆Hr(X,B).


若 Hr−1​(X,Z) 自由，则 Qξ​ 是使 q∗​G 中性的初始系数商。


对有限族 (Gv​)，有规范短正合列
0→⋂v​Kv​∑v​Kv​​→⋂v​Kv​A​→∑v​Kv​A​→0.


若 Hr​(X,Z)≅Zβ，可实现的 Kξ​ 恰为至多由 β 个元素生成的子群。



在 r=3 时，这是有限带 2-gerbe 的定理。可进一步给出标准对象上的条件性应用：若一个 torsion Chern–Simons 或 String lifting bundle 2-gerbe 已配备 μn​-带细化，则 Kξ​ 是其“三维循环可检测部分”，而 Qξ​ 是消去该部分的初始带商。
(b) 是否为具名开放问题
这不是文献中的具名开放问题。它是一个外部自然、但本质上属于“UCT 加高阶 gerbe 分类”的统一定理。
高阶 bundle 2-gerbe 的困难文献主要关心弱模型、刚性化、连接、String 结构及几何平凡化，而不是其同调像商。Roberts–Vozzo 明确区分弱 bundle 2-gerbe、rigidification 与稳定同构，并指出稳定刚性化需要额外的 universal diffeological model；单纯的分类类并不解决该几何问题。arXiv 因而不能把上述 UCT 定理宣传为对 rigidification 问题的推进。
(c) 现有方法可直接承担的部分
几乎全部代数部分可逐次数平移：


UCT 中
0→Ext1(Hr−1​,A)→Hr(X,A)→Hom(Hr​,A)→0;


同调像、余核与字符湮灭子；


初始系数商；


多分量交、和及短正合列；


有限商映射的精确纤维基数；


Hr​≅Zβ 时的生成元数分类；


在 ⋁βSr 上的有限神经与好覆盖实现。


本文关于 Kω​、纯 Ext 盲区及初始商的论证已经明确不依赖 UCT 的非规范分裂。 
(d) 缺失结构与失败机制
缺失内容集中于高阶几何，而不在代数：


2-stackification 或一般高阶 stackification；


A-带状 2-gerbe 的稳定等价与 H3(−,A) 分类接口；


显式 Čech 三余循环到 2-gerbe 的构造；


高阶中性判据及 coefficient pushforward；


多分量概念在 2-groupoid 或 ∞-stack 中的正确替代；


若应用于 Chern–Simons/String 对象，还需处理连接、微分上同调及 U(1) 而非有限常带。


最可能的失败不是定理错误，而是外部意义不足。对于标准 Chern–Simons 2-gerbe，带通常为 U(1)，特征类为整数四类；有限带 μn​ 细化往往需要 torsion 条件和额外选择。不同 μn​-lift 可能给出不同 Kξ​，使所谓“String 同调像”不是原对象的规范不变量。若不能消除此选择依赖，结果仍将是展示相对量，重复本文当前的主要局限。
(e) 概率与影响
成功概率：65%。
纯定理层面成功概率较高；但若只完成次数平移，其学术影响有限，适合 Theory and Applications of Categories、Journal of Homotopy and Related Structures 或 Applied Categorical Structures。只有在标准 lifting 2-gerbe 上证明真正规范、与连接或稳定等价兼容的应用后，才可能达到 Algebraic & Geometric Topology 附近的层级。
研究规模：一篇后续论文可补齐，但必须移除自定义语义层，直接在高阶栈或 ∞-topos 中陈述。

3. 边界候选：纯 Ext 提升障碍与同伦群扩张类的同一性
(a) 可望证明的精确定理
Neeb–Wagemann–Wockel 的 Problem 8.2(a) 询问如下等式。
设 X 单连通，P→X 为主 K-丛，并满足
∂2P​=0,∂3P​=0.
于是 π2​(P) 是 π2​(X) 被 π1​(K) 的交换扩张。若
1→Z→K→K→1
中 Z 为 K(Γ,1)-群，则 ∂2K​:π2​(K)→Γ 给出推前扩张类
(∂2K​)∗​[π2​(P)]∈Ext(π2​(X),Γ).
目标定理为
obsP​(K)=(∂2K​)∗​[π2​(P)]
在
Λ3(X,Γ)≅Ext(π2​(X),Γ)
中成立。
Problem 8.2(b) 则要求证明对 ∂2K​=0 的情形，
obsP​(K)=(∂2P​)∗[π1​(K)].
两问均载于同文 §8，Problem 8.2，accepted-version p. 37。arXiv
(b) 开放状态
原文明确列为问题；本次检索未发现完整解决这两个等式的后续论文。由于这类结果可能以 Postnikov 不变量、Serre 谱序列或 crossed-module 分类的不同术语出现，开放状态的判断置信度低于 Problem 8.1(b)，应表述为“未检得解决”，而非绝对断言。
(c) 现有工具可承担的部分
本文只能承担“定位”而不能承担“识别”。
次数平移后的 UCT 可证明：在题设下，障碍类位于
Ext1(H2​(X),Γ)⊂H3(X,Γ),
且其 H3​-评价为零。本文关于纯 Ext 类对同调评价及全部有限字符不可见的结论，恰好说明为什么现有 Kω​ 体系无法进一步区分这些类。现稿已经明确证明：非零纯 Ext 类可具有零同调像，并且所有字符均不能检测它。
因此，现有工具可以证明两边落在同一 Ext 群、具有相同的全部“可见性零数据”，却不能证明两边相等。
(d) 缺失结构与反例机制
需要增加的不是一个普通引理，而是一整套新的比较机制：


主丛同伦长正合列的链级模型；


π2​(P) 的交换扩张类与 Moore–Postnikov k-不变量的对应；


lifting gerbe 或 lifting 2-gerbe 类与该 k-不变量的比较；


Serre 谱序列中的 transgression 与 crossed-module 扩张类的兼容性；


所有构造在改变基点、截面和提升时的自然性。


最可能的失败机制是存在未被单纯扩张类记录的 secondary correction，例如 Whitehead 乘积、Postnikov 三类或弱 crossed-module coherence。即使最终等式成立，本文现有的同调像和字符对偶也无法发现这些修正项。
(e) 概率与影响
成功概率：18%。
若能同时解决 Problem 8.2(a)、(b)，并进一步处理 Problem 8.3 中 universal-cover pullback 的核，成果可达到较强的代数拓扑或无限维 Lie 理论专门期刊层级。单独证明一个受限情形的影响较小。
研究规模：需要新的研究纲领，而非本文工具箱的直接延伸。

不应作为主要机会的具名问题
1. Period–index 问题
此方向与 gerbe 的联系表面上最强，实际上最容易造成过度声称。
截至 2026 年 8 月，经典及一般 unramified period–index conjecture 已被明确的三维光滑射影反例否定；当前问题已经转向修正后的界、素数限制及特殊几何类别。arXiv Hyperkähler 情形的强化猜想
ind(α)∣per(α)dimX/2
仍只在若干类别和非特殊 coprime Brauer 类上成立。arXiv
本文体系只能读取一个有限带 H2 类的 UCT 评价及系数商，而 ind(α) 是存在最小秩 twisted sheaf、Azumaya algebra 或 Brauer–Severi variety 的问题。它依赖 twisted K-theory、Hodge 类、稳定模空间及更高谱序列微分。两个具有相同 H2​-评价乃至相同完整 cohomology class 的 gerbe 展示，仍可能在允许的 twisted module 秩方面表现不同。
因此，该方法体系不能实质攻击 period–index 问题。把 A/Kω​ 称为“index 的近似”没有数学依据。
2. 哪些三次整上同调类可由 small bundle gerbe 表示
Mathai–Melrose 明确提出：刻画紧流形上哪些
H3(M,Z)
类可由定义在光滑、紧、有限维纤维丛上的 small bundle gerbe 表示，仍是开放问题。arXiv
本文的有限交叠站点和余循环 prestack 只产生有限维 Čech 展示；它不产生一个光滑紧纤维丛
F⟶M
使给定三类在 F 上平凡，也不控制总空间紧性、纤维几何或相关 twisted index。有限好覆盖的开集并不自动组成 small gerbe 所要求的紧纤维丛。
故此问题虽与 gerbe 更直接，现有工具仍未触及其决定性几何条件。

本稿当前的诚实学术上限
现稿不是空洞改名，也不完全等同于综述。至少有三部分具有独立技术内容：


对 sheafification 的 plus–plus 表示及 separatedness 所需位置作了细致处理；


得到了 pullback-stable 代表选择与终端本质满射之间的刚性结论；


给出了显式余循环 prestack，实现预定分量类及同调像，并完成所选 ⋁S2 数据上的有限群存在分类。


但这些贡献的共同性质是：它们均发生在由作者提供站点、带、提升及比较数据的模型类内部。 现稿自己也明确说明构造定理是固定有限神经和 typed-expansion 框架内的内部存在结果。 
此外，Kω​ 与 Qω​ 只沿指定比较 zigzag 传输，不具有路径无关性；不同 zigzag 可以产生不同带自同构。  经验模型部分则证明了规范提升必然分裂，并明确排除了向 AMB 障碍和裸经验模型分类的转移。
因此，最准确的定位是：

一篇包含若干正确而细致的有限站点构造与 UCT 分类结果的专门研究论文，但核心不变量尚未附着到既有标准对象，因而概念影响受限。

若沿用所述 TIER 语言，我会将其定位为：
upper TIER-4 / lower TIER-3 specialist mathematics​
而不是纯粹的 TIER-4 综述，因为显式实现定理及有限群充要分类并非简单复述；但也尚未达到通常意义上的 TIER-2，因为最强结论仍是为预先选择的数据作存在分类，而不是发现标准对象的新性质。
标准术语的修订消除了可读性障碍，却不会改变这一数学上限。APAL 的实质适配仍然较弱：本文没有得到一个对标准逻辑、标准经验模型或标准语义类别产生新后果的逻辑定理。

投稿层级与具体期刊
按当前内容整体投稿
最现实的目标是中低层专门期刊，而非综合性逻辑或高层纯数学期刊。
第一选择：Applied Categorical Structures
该刊明确接受范畴论方法在几何、拓扑、物理和计算机科学中的应用，因而能够容纳 presheaf、stack、gerbe 与语义接口的混合结构。施普林格自然 在不新增外部定理的情况下，这是当前稿件最合理的正式目标。
第二选择：Cahiers de Topologie et Géométrie Différentielle Catégoriques
若保留完整的站点、栈化和带状 gerbe 结构，并降低对量子语境性的宣传，该刊的专业范围较相称。其学术层级不会被误认为对现稿贡献的过高估计。
有条件选择：Theory and Applications of Categories
TAC 的范围包括高阶范畴以及范畴方法在代数、几何、拓扑和数学物理中的应用。MTA TAC 但该刊要求工作对范畴方法本身有显著推进。现稿若整体保留大量自定义语义层，接受概率有限；只有在重组为“component gerbes of a stack with prescribed component sheaf”及有限站点实现定理后，才是合理候选。这样的重组改善适配度，不构成数学层级提升。
不建议当前直接投：Journal of Homotopy and Related Structures
该刊覆盖同伦、同调代数、拓扑群、范畴结构及相关几何。College Publications 但现稿当前的主线不是标准对象上的同伦结果。若完成候选 1，或至少完成候选 2 的标准高阶 gerbe 版本，则该刊会成为适当目标。
不建议当前投：Journal of Pure and Applied Algebra
该刊强调具有一般数学意义或直接应用的代数结果。科学直通车 当前的展示相对商与自选模型类分类尚不足以支持这一定位。完成具名 Problem 8.1(b)，或建立真正展示无关的高阶 lifting-gerbe 定理后，才值得考虑。

最终建议
本稿本身不应再试图从 contextuality 获得外部重要性。最有价值的研究决策是将后续工作集中于：
τ([cK→G​])=±δ1​(G→G/K)​
这一 crossed-module 特征类与提升 gerbe 类的链级比较。它是唯一既有准确文献出处、又与现有 Čech 二余循环和 gerbe 障碍工具直接相接、且核心缺口可由一篇后续论文解决的具名问题。
若该比较无法完成，则次优但可靠的成果是有限带高阶 gerbe 的次数统一 UCT 商定理；其学术价值在于把现稿从作者设计的语义对象迁移到标准高阶栈对象，而不在于再次复制 ⋁Sr 上的有限群分类。

\section{Homological evaluation and presentation-relative quotients}
\label{sec:homological-visibility}

We now fix one global component section together with a finite nerve representative. The quotient attached to a chosen cocycle still depends on that presentation, so in order to isolate the part determined by the cohomology class itself we pass to the homological evaluation map. Throughout the section we work with one fixed finite cover and the comparison maps coming from the identification of \v{C}ech cochains with simplicial cochains on its finite nerve. The resulting homological image subgroup and quotient are presentation-relative invariants only relative to the homology-equivalent changes of presentation singled out below.

We also assume that the chosen cover $\mathcal U$ trivializes the band, so that the coefficients may be viewed as a constant finite abelian group $A$ on the nerve. The case of nontrivial monodromy, which would require local coefficients, is not considered here.

\subsection{Finite nerve representatives}

\begin{definition}[Finite nerve representative of the obstruction class]\label{def:finite-nerve-presentation}
Fix an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, and assume conditions \textup{(i)}--\textup{(iv)} of \suppresult{Finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative}, with the ordered \v{C}ech convention of \suppresult{Ordered \v{C}ech convention}. A \emph{finite nerve representative} of the component obstruction class consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a finite covering family
\[
\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I},
\qquad
a=[r]_p,
\]
whose \v{C}ech nerve $N(\mathcal U)$ is finite;
\item a trivialization of the band on $\mathcal U$, so that the band may be viewed as a constant coefficient finite abelian group $A$ on $N(\mathcal U)$;
\item the induced finite-nerve class
\[
\omega\in H^2(N(\mathcal U),A),
\]
obtained by identifying \v{C}ech cochains on $\mathcal U$ with simplicial cochains on the finite nerve, transporting the component obstruction cocycle through that identification, and writing
\[
\omega:=\omega_v
\]
for the chosen global component section. When the class is also viewed as a derived
$H^2$-class, the presentation includes either the
\v{C}ech-to-derived comparison hypothesis of
\Cref{def:cech-derived-comparison-package} or the finite good-cover Leray
comparison of \suppresult{Finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve, Leray comparison, and one-state admissible expansion}. Without one of
these comparison inputs, \(\omega\) is only the selected finite-nerve
\v{C}ech/simplicial class.
\end{enumerate}
Write
\[
\eta_A:H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)\longrightarrow \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)
\]
for the map in the universal coefficient exact sequence
\[
0\to \operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\to H^2(N(\mathcal U),A)\xrightarrow{\eta_A}\mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A)\to 0
\]
\cite[Sec.~3.1]{Hatcher2002}, and define the \emph{homological evaluation map}
\[
\mathrm{ev}_{\omega}:=\eta_A(\omega).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Presentation-comparison groupoid]\label{def:finite-nerve-comparison-relation}
For finite nerve representatives of a fixed component obstruction class, an
\emph{elementary comparison} is one of the three operations listed in
\Cref{prop:invariance-visible-quotients}: homology-equivalent refinement,
cohomologous cocycle replacement, or re-trivialization of the constant band.
The \emph{presentation-comparison groupoid} has these presentations as
objects and finite zigzags of elementary comparisons and formal inverses as
morphisms, with composition given by concatenation of zigzags. We say that two
presentations are comparison-equivalent when they lie in the same connected
component of this groupoid. Thus the comparison data carried by a finite
zigzag are part of the pseudofunctorial assignment; no path independence
between distinct zigzags is asserted unless an additional coherence
hypothesis is imposed.
\end{definition}


 succeeded in 257ms:
\subsection{Homological images, cokernels, and annihilating characters}

\begin{definition}[Homological image and cokernel]\label{def:intrinsic-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, define the \emph{presentation-relative homological image subgroup}
\[
K_{\omega}:=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})
\]
and the associated \emph{homological cokernel}
\[
Q_{\omega}:=\operatorname{coker}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})=A/K_{\omega}.
\]
These terms are relative to the presentation-comparison groupoid of
\Cref{def:finite-nerve-comparison-relation}; no presentation-free quotient is
asserted outside that groupoid.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Class-trivializing characters]\label{def:class-admissible-character}
In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}, let
\[
X_{\omega}:=\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi_*(\omega)=0\},
\qquad
\widehat A:=\mathrm{Hom}(A,\mathbb T).
\]
Elements of $X_{\omega}$ are called \emph{class-trivializing} for $\omega$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Character-theoretic detection of the homological cokernel]\label{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:class-admissible-character},
\[
X_{\omega}
=
\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi\circ \mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0\}
=
\mathrm{Ann}(K_{\omega}).
\]
Consequently,
\[
K_{\omega}
=
\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi,
\qquad
Q_{\omega}\cong A\Big/\!\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For every character $\chi:A\to\mathbb T$, naturality of the universal coefficient sequence gives a commutative square
\[
\begin{CD}
H^2(N(\mathcal U),A) @>\eta_A>> \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),A) \\
@V\chi_*VV @VV(\chi\circ -)V \\
H^2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb T) @>\eta_{\mathbb T}>> \mathrm{Hom}(H_2(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),\mathbb T).
\end{CD}
\]
Since $\mathbb T$ is divisible and hence injective in $\mathbf{Ab}$, one has
\[
\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z),\mathbb T)=0
\]
\cite[Sec.~2.3]{Weibel1994}. Thus $\eta_{\mathbb T}$ is an isomorphism by \Cref{def:finite-nerve-presentation}. Therefore
\[
\chi_*(\omega)=0
\Longleftrightarrow
\eta_{\mathbb T}(\chi_*(\omega))=0
\Longleftrightarrow
\chi\circ \eta_A(\omega)=0
\Longleftrightarrow
\chi\circ \mathrm{ev}_{\omega}=0.
\]
This proves the first displayed identity, and the second is simply the definition of the annihilator of a subgroup of $A$.

Let $H:=K_{\omega}=\mathrm{Im}(\mathrm{ev}_{\omega})\le A$. By the first part of the proof, $X_{\omega}=\mathrm{Ann}(H)$. Finite abelian duality implies
\[
\bigcap_{\chi\in \mathrm{Ann}(H)}\ker\chi=H
\]
\cite[Part~I, Chaps.~2, 12]{Terras1999}. Hence
\[
K_{\omega}
=
\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\]
The final isomorphism follows immediately:
\[
Q_{\omega}
=
A/K_{\omega}
\cong
A\Big/\!\bigcap_{\chi\in X_{\omega}}\ker\chi.
\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Character detection of the homological cokernel]\label{cor:intrinsic-character-detection}
The quotient map
\[
\pi_{\omega}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\omega}

 succeeded in 257ms:
\subsection{The exact interface with empirical models}

Abramsky--Brandenburger empirical models already have two categorical
reformulations close to the question considered here. Car\`u identifies the
degree-one cohomology used by Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa with torsors
trivialized by the measurement cover \cite[Prop.~6.3]{Caru2017}. More recently,
Barbosa, Kharoof, and Okay construct fully faithful functors from measurement
scenarios to bundle and simplicial-bundle scenarios, together with natural
isomorphisms on their empirical-model functors
\cite{BarbosaKharoofOkay2024}. Neither construction produces a banded gerbe
or a banded stack lift in the sense of
\Cref{def:banded-stack-lift}. The following comparison locates the
gap precisely.

Let a \emph{finite measurement scenario} be a finite set \(X\), finite
nonempty outcome sets \(O_x\) for \(x\in X\), and a finite antichain
\(\mathcal M\) of subsets of \(X\) whose union is \(X\). Write
\(\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}\) for the finite context category consisting of
the subcontexts of members of \(\mathcal M\), with inclusions as arrows, and
equip it with the coverage induced by the maximal contexts. We also use its
terminal augmentation, obtained by adjoining \(X\) and declaring
\(\mathcal M\) to cover \(X\). A finite empirical model
\[
e=(e_C)_{C\in\mathcal M}
\]
is a compatible family of probability distributions on
\(\prod_{x\in C}O_x\). Its support presheaf is
\[
S_e(U)=\operatorname{supp}(e_U)
\qquad(U\in\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}),
\]
where compatibility makes the marginal \(e_U\) independent of the maximal
context containing \(U\) \cite{AbramskyBrandenburger2011}. At the adjoined
terminal object we take the right Kan extension
\[
\widehat S_e(X):=
\varprojlim_{U\in\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}}S_e(U).
\]
Thus an element of \(\widehat S_e(X)\) is, by definition, a compatible family
of supported local assignments. Below \(aS_e\) denotes the sheafification of
this terminally augmented presheaf.

A \emph{context refinement} used below is a refinement
\(\rho:\mathcal M'\to\mathcal M\) on the same measurements and outcomes,
with \(C'\subseteq\rho(C')\), for which marginalization defines the refined
model \(\rho^*e\). The inclusion of downward-closed context categories induces
a functor
\[
u_\rho:\mathcal D_{\mathcal M'}\hookrightarrow
\mathcal D_{\mathcal M}
\]
which is required to be continuous for the two generated cover topologies and
to extend to their terminal augmentations. We also require the natural
base-change isomorphism
\[
S_{\rho^*e}\cong u_\rho^*S_e.
\]
This includes ordinary iterated refinements which are covering refinements
for the generated topology. No
claim is made here for resource-theoretic simulations which change the
measurement or outcome sets.

\begin{proposition}[Abramsky--Brandenburger support interface]\label{prop:ab-compatibility-translation}
For every finite empirical model \(e\), the following sets are naturally in
bijection:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item compatible families \((s_C)_{C\in\mathcal M}\) with
      \(s_C\in S_e(C)\);
\item global assignments \(s\in\prod_{x\in X}O_x\) such that
      \(s|_C\in S_e(C)\) for every \(C\in\mathcal M\);
\item global sections of the support presheaf in the standard
      Abramsky--Brandenburger sense;
\item elements of \(\widehat S_e(X)\), equivalently elements of
      \((aS_e)(X)\) on the terminally augmented context site.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, if both \(\CompSec_s(r)\) and \(\Sec_s(r)\) are interpreted
from this same support presheaf on the standard measurement cover, then
\[
M,p\Vdash\CompSec_s(r)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
M,p\Vdash\Sec_s(r).
\]
In particular, strong contextuality is absence of a compatible family at the
set-valued level; it is not the matching-without-amalgamation case
\(\CompSec_s(r)\land\neg\Sec_s(r)\) of this paper.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The event presheaf
\(\mathcal E(U)=\prod_{x\in U}O_x\) is a sheaf. Hence a compatible family
\((s_C)\) glues uniquely to a global assignment \(s\), and its restrictions
remain in the prescribed supports. Conversely, restriction of such an
assignment gives a compatible supported family. This proves the first three
identifications. The fourth is the defining limit formula for
\(\widehat S_e(X)\); the plus construction for the cover
\(\mathcal M\to X\) gives the same set in the sheafification. The displayed
equivalence follows immediately.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Support versus probabilistic contextuality]\label{rem:support-vs-probabilistic-contextuality}
The proposition concerns the possibilistic support of \(e\). Probabilistic
noncontextuality asks instead for a distribution on
\(\prod_{x\in X}O_x\) whose marginals are the given distributions \(e_C\).
That is a fibre-extension problem for the distribution presheaf, and is not
determined by \(S_e\): empirical models with the same support can have
different probabilistic noncontextuality behaviour.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Canonical split stack and the degree obstruction]\label{thm:empirical-model-stack-interface}
Let \(e\) be a finite empirical model on the context site above.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item There is a canonical banded stack lift with zero band,
\[
\mathfrak E_e^0:=\operatorname{Disc}(aS_e),
\qquad

 succeeded in 258ms:
\section{Aggregation over component sections}\label{sec:branch-aggregation}

We compare the quotients attached to the different global component sections of a single admissible parameter. Retaining the component label leads to a label-aware quotient, while forgetting it leads to a common-character quotient. The comparison results below are formal once the family of componentwise homological image subgroups have been fixed, whereas the construction theorems later in the section remain internal to explicitly chosen finite nerves and constant bands.

\begin{definition}[Family of componentwise homological image subgroups]\label{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}
Fix an admissible parameter $r:\mathsf{Ref}_s$ at a state $p$, and assume:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the hypotheses of \Cref{def:cech-gluing-obstruction} hold;
\item the set $\Vis_{p,s}(r)$ is finite and nonempty;
\item every component gerbe $\mathfrak G_v$ is banded by the same finite abelian group $A$ and admits a finite nerve representative of its component obstruction class $\omega_v$.
\end{enumerate}
For each global component section $v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)$, write
\[
K_v:=K_{\omega_v},
\qquad
Q_v:=Q_{\omega_v}=A/K_v,
\qquad
\pi_v:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_v.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Label-aware quotient]\label{thm:branch-sensitive-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, consider the diagonal map
\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}}:A\longrightarrow \prod_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} Q_v,
\qquad
x\longmapsto (\pi_v(x))_v.
\]
Then
\[
\ker(\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}})=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
\]
Consequently, the quotient
\[
Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r):=A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
\]
is canonically isomorphic to $\mathrm{Im}(\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}})$. It is the maximal quotient of $A$ detected by the family of componentwise quotient maps when the component label is retained.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $x\in A$ one has $\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}}(x)=0$ if and only if $\pi_v(x)=0$ for every global component section $v$, which is equivalent to $x\in K_v$ for every $v$. Hence
\[
\ker(\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}})=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
\]
The first isomorphism theorem therefore yields
\[
\mathrm{Im}(\Delta_{\mathrm{lab}})
\cong
A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
\]
Thus, once the global component section is known, two elements of $A$ have the same image exactly when they have the same image in every componentwise quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Common-character quotient]\label{thm:branch-uniform-visible-quotient}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, let
\[
X_v:=\{\chi\in \widehat A\mid \chi_*(\omega_v)=0\}
\]
for each global component section $v$, and define the common annihilating characters by
\[
X^{\mathrm{com}}(r):=\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} X_v.
\]
Then
\[
X^{\mathrm{com}}(r)=\mathrm{Ann}\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr).
\]
Consequently, the quotient
\[
Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r):=A\Big/\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
\]
is exactly the quotient detected by the characters that annihilate every componentwise homological image simultaneously.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \Cref{thm:intrinsic-visible-quotient}, one has
\[
X_v=\mathrm{Ann}(K_v)
\]
for every $v$. Therefore
\[
X^{\mathrm{com}}(r)
=
\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} \mathrm{Ann}(K_v)
=
\mathrm{Ann}\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr),
\]
where the last identity is the standard annihilator formula for finite abelian groups \cite[Part~I, Chap.~2]{Terras1999}. Finite abelian duality then gives
\[
\bigcap_{\chi\in X^{\mathrm{com}}(r)} \ker\chi
=
\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
\]
Hence the quotient detected by all common annihilating characters is precisely
\[
A\Big/\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v.
\qedhere
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Canonical comparison and component-forgetting kernel]\label{def:branch-comparison-map}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branchwise-intrinsic-data}, let
\[
\pi_{\mathrm{lab}}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\pi_{\mathrm{com}}:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)
\]
be the canonical quotient maps. Since
\[
\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v
\subseteq
\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v,
\]
there is a unique surjective homomorphism
\[
\beta_r:Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)
\]
such that
\[
\beta_r\circ \pi_{\mathrm{lab}}=\pi_{\mathrm{com}}.
\]
Its kernel is denoted
\[
K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r):=
\ker(\beta_r)
\cong
\Bigl(\sum_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr)\Big/\Bigl(\bigcap_{v\in \Vis_{p,s}(r)} K_v\Bigr)
\]
and called the \emph{component-forgetting kernel}.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact comparison of the aggregate quotients]\label{thm:branch-comparison-exact-sequence}
In the setting of \Cref{def:branch-comparison-map}, there is a short exact sequence
\[
0\longrightarrow K_{\mathrm{forg}}(r)\longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{lab}}(r)\xrightarrow{\ \beta_r\ } Q_{\mathrm{com}}(r)\longrightarrow 0.
\]

+Please act as a hostile but constructive external referee for the attached main.pdf, "Component Gerbes and Homological Quotients over Teams and Presheaves." I need a publication decision, a priority audit, and a go/no-go decision on one specific research extension. A hard rejection with precise reasons is more useful than a soft acceptance. For every objection, state what evidence, theorem, rewrite, or deletion would resolve it; if nothing realistically would, say that.
+
+The editorial and review history materially affects the question. This paper was rejected by Annals of Pure and Applied Logic. The editor's stated grounds were presentation, not mathematics: opaque coined terminology and excessive length. Both stated grounds have now been addressed. A terminology audit found 68 coined-term families: 29 collided with established meanings and 27 were opaque. The manuscript was renamed throughout into standard categorical and logical vocabulary. The article was reduced from 96 pages to 48 pages, with 50 pages moved into a separately compiled supplement; the theorem count is unchanged.
+
+You previously reviewed the mathematics of this same project. Your judgment was that the paper's own main classification openly depends on a pre-chosen good cover, a finite nerve, a constant finite band, and a non-split lifting. You therefore classified it as an organization of standard inputs, several presentation-relative quotients, a construction theorem, and a finite-group classification, not as a new general gerbe-classification principle. You also observed that the top model class admits site, presheaf, prestack, and stack data satisfying the type conditions directly as expansions. That is a model-class convention, not an existence or closure theorem deriving the added objects from pre-existing lower-layer models. Nothing below retracts or conceals either judgment. Please hold the revised paper to those limits.
+
+Only main.pdf is attached to this request. The unprovided 50-page supplement contains proofs and constructions moved out of the article, so its relevant statements are recorded below. Please assess the article and the stated supplement results together, but do not assume any stronger result than the hypotheses explicitly written here.
+
+STANDARD INPUTS IMPORTED AND CITED, NOT CLAIMED AS OURS
+
+1. Universal satisfaction on nonempty teams of assignments, singleton-team agreement with ordinary Tarskian semantics, and persistence under team refinement are standard team-semantic facts.
+
+2. Presheaves, the plus-plus construction of sheafification, separatedness, stackification of categories fibred in groupoids, and the identification of the component sheaf of a stackification with the sheafification of the presheaf of components are standard sheaf and stack theory. The relevant sources are Mac Lane--Moerdijk and the Stacks Project.
+
+3. Classification of abelian-band gerbes by Giraud H^2, neutrality as existence of a global object, the local-object/overlap-isomorphism/triple-overlap-defect construction of a Cech 2-cocycle, change by coboundary, and transport under banded equivalence are standard Giraud--Breen--Brylinski--Murray gerbe theory.
+
+4. The Cech-to-derived passage is never claimed without an explicit comparison isomorphism or a Leray good-cover hypothesis. The nerve lemma, open-star good covers of barycentric subdivisions, and the identification of ordered Cech cochains with simplicial cochains on the selected finite nerve are standard.
+
+5. The universal coefficient exact sequence
+
+   0 -> Ext^1(H_1(N;Z),A) -> H^2(N;A) -> Hom(H_2(N;Z),A) -> 0,
+
+   its naturality, injectivity of the divisible group T, Pontryagin character separation for finite abelian groups, elementary subgroup identities, and the structure theorem for finite abelian groups are imported algebraic inputs.
+
+6. The Abramsky--Brandenburger support-presheaf interface is imported. The Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa obstruction is the relative Cech H^1 class gamma_e(s) of a presheaf, equivalently a torsor class indexed by a chosen local section; Caru's torsor account and the later Barbosa--Kharoof--Okay comparison results are prior work. We do not identify that H^1 class with our H^2 gerbe class.
+
+WHAT THE REVISED PAPER CLAIMS AS ITS OWN CONTRIBUTION
+
+The originality claim is deliberately limited to: organizing those inputs into the stated typed semantic framework; isolating exact hypotheses under which matching without amalgamation is equivalent to non-neutral component gerbes; a particular presentation-comparison groupoid and its presentation-relative homological image/cokernel; the two finite-family aggregate quotients and their comparison; explicit finite-site prestacks realizing prescribed component classes and homological images; the resulting subgroup and finite-group existence classifications; a restricted lower-language separation theorem; a terminal-fibre rigidity theorem for strictly pullback-stable representatives; and the exact negative comparison with bare empirical models. The quotient and exact-sequence algebra is presented as formal once its subgroup data are fixed. We do not claim a new general classification of gerbes, a presentation-free invariant, a classification of empirical models, or an unconditional construction of non-split lifts from lower-layer objects.
+
+Here are the principal results, with their full operative hypotheses.
+
+A. Semantic and sheafification results, mostly supporting material.
+
+An information state is p=(Gamma,R), with R a nonempty team of assignments in the finite context Gamma, and M,p |= phi means that every assignment in R satisfies phi in ordinary Tarskian semantics. The map from point assignments to singleton teams preserves satisfaction. The higher layers L_1 <= L_2 <= L_3 <= L_4 are truth-preserving reduct expansions: each is the class of supplied expansions by data of the stipulated types, and lower-language atomic clauses are interpreted through the reduct. This is explicitly a definition of broad generated model classes, not an expansion-existence or closure theorem for an independently given restricted class.
+
+For an admissible parameter r at p, let a=[r]_p, let C_a be the pullback-based slice site, and let F:C_a^op -> Set be the supplied presheaf of local sections. If F is separated at every object of C_a, then a strict matching family on a cover of a exists if and only if (aF)(a) is nonempty. The forward implication needs no separatedness; the reverse uses the plus-plus representation and separatedness on every cover member, pullback overlap, and refinement, not merely at a. Thus sheafification removes matching-without-amalgamation for this separated restriction.
+
+The supplement also proves a deliberately narrow undefinability result: in one explicit model there are four admissible reference elements that are indistinguishable by every parameter-free, constant-free, automorphism-invariant one-variable formula of the Sigma_1 reduct, while respectively realizing (matching and global), (matching but no global), (local but no matching), and (no local section). Hence the six corresponding local/global failure predicates are not definable on that admissible subdomain in that restricted fragment. No claim is made for formulas with parameters, tuples, the Sigma_2 presheaf structure, or arbitrary lower languages.
+
+B. Component gerbes and the matching-without-amalgamation criterion.
+
+Let E be an A-banded stack on C_a equipped with an isomorphism pi_0(E) ~= aF. For every global component section v in (aF)(a), the full substack E[v] on objects with component v is an A-banded gerbe. Its Giraud class defines
+
+   delta_E:(aF)(a) -> H^2(C_a,A),    v |-> [E[v]],
+
+and [E[v]]=0 iff E[v] is neutral iff E[v](a) is nonempty. The map is transported by a banded equivalence E -> E' compatible with the component-sheaf identifications and the induced band isomorphism. This is a componentwise packaging of standard gerbe classification, not a new classification theorem.
+
+For the comparison with the original presheaf predicate, the paper separately assumes: (H1) a chosen banded groupoid lift P_r of F and its stackification L_r; (H2) a fixed abelian band sheaf A on C_a; (H3) essential surjectivity P_r(a) -> L_r(a) on the terminal fibre; and separatedness of F on all of C_a. Under exactly these assumptions,
+
+   matching without an amalgamation in F(a)
+
+holds iff (aF)(a) is nonempty and every component gerbe G_v is non-neutral. For the equivalent formulation by nonzero derived classes omega_v, the additional hypothesis (H4) is required: for every v there is a filtered cofinal family of covers whose needed intersections trivialize the band and for which colim_U Cech H^2(U,A) -> H^2(C_a,A) is an isomorphism (a Leray condition is a sufficient verification). Without H4, only the cover-relative Cech class is asserted.
+
+C. Terminal-fibre rigidity from the supplement.
+
+A representative-rigid lift means that one representative of each presheaf section is chosen and preserved strictly by every pullback. Assume such an A-banded groupoid lift P_r, assume F is separated at every object of C_a, and write u:F -> aF. Essential surjectivity on the terminal fibre implies that u_a is surjective. Conversely, if H^1(C_a,A)=0, surjectivity of u_a implies terminal essential surjectivity. Under that H^1-vanishing, the following are equivalent: terminal essential surjectivity; u_a is bijective; and F satisfies the sheaf gluing condition at a. Consequently a separated presheaf with matching without amalgamation cannot have a representative-rigid lift that is terminally essentially surjective. The paper treats this as a rigidity result showing that a useful non-split lift must contain genuinely noncanonical lift data.
+
+D. Finite-nerve homological image and cokernel.
+
+Fix one global component v, one finite cover U with finite nerve N, a trivialization of its band as a constant finite abelian group A, and a selected finite-nerve class omega in H^2(N;A). If omega is also called a derived component class, H4 or the explicit finite good-cover Leray theorem is assumed. Let
+
+   ev_omega:H_2(N;Z) -> A
+
+be the UCT evaluation, K_omega=Im(ev_omega), and Q_omega=A/K_omega. These are only presentation-relative. They transport along a specified groupoid freely generated by: refinements whose nerve map induces an H_2-isomorphism and pulls back omega; cohomologous cocycle replacement; band retrivialization; and formal inverses. Different zigzags may induce different band automorphisms; no path independence or invariant outside this comparison groupoid is claimed.
+
+For X_omega={chi in Hom(A,T): chi_*(omega)=0}, UCT naturality and Ext^1(H_1(N),T)=0 give
+
+   X_omega=Ann(K_omega),
+   K_omega=intersection_{chi in X_omega} ker(chi),
+   Q_omega ~= A/K_omega.
+
+For every surjection q:A -> B, q factors uniquely through A -> Q_omega iff q kills K_omega iff q_*(omega) has zero H_2-evaluation iff q_*(omega) lies in Ext^1(H_1(N;Z),B). If H_1(N;Z)=0, these are also equivalent to q_*(omega)=0. A class has K_omega=0 exactly when it lies in the UCT Ext^1 kernel. These are UCT and finite-duality consequences attached to the selected presentation; the paper's contribution is their organization and use, not the underlying algebra.
+
+For a chosen 2-cocycle alpha, the supplement also defines the coordinate subgroup H_alpha generated by all displayed values alpha_ijk and the quotient A/H_alpha. It proves K_omega is contained in H_alpha and that A/K_omega surjects onto A/H_alpha. This coordinate quotient is explicitly cocycle-representative-dependent.
+
+E. Finite-family aggregation.
+
+Assume a finite nonempty set V of global component sections, a common finite abelian band A, and a finite-nerve representative for every omega_v. Put K_v=Im(ev_omega_v). The label-aware and common-character quotients are
+
+   Q_lab=A/(intersection_v K_v),
+   Q_com=A/(sum_v K_v).
+
+The canonical map Q_lab -> Q_com sits in the short exact sequence
+
+   0 -> (sum_v K_v)/(intersection_v K_v)
+     -> Q_lab -> Q_com -> 0.
+
+The kernel is called the component-forgetting kernel. This is elementary finite-group algebra once the K_v are supplied. The associated minimum auxiliary-register cardinalities are just exact fibre cardinalities: |K_omega| for one quotient, |(sum K_v)/(intersection K_v)| for the comparison, and max_v |K_v| for one common register separating every componentwise quotient.
+
+F. Explicit finite-site construction and subgroup classification, from the supplement.
+
+Let U be a finite good-cover presentation with connected finite nerve N, and let C_U be the finite intersection site, including a strict initial object for empty intersections. Let A_U be the genuine constant sheaf associated with a finite abelian group A, assume H_1(N;Z)=0, and use the specified Leray comparison. For a finite nonempty label set Lambda and arbitrary homomorphisms
+
+   phi_lambda:H_2(N;Z) -> A,
+
+the supplement explicitly constructs a presheaf F and an A_U-banded prestack P by normalized simplicial 2-cocycles. In the broad typed-expansion model class, its stackification has global components indexed by Lambda, component class omega_lambda with ev_omega_lambda=phi_lambda and K_lambda=Im(phi_lambda), and neutral components exactly where phi_lambda=0. The prestack is terminally essentially surjective, with the empty-to-empty case counted as vacuous when no component is neutral. Matching always exists; matching without amalgamation holds iff every phi_lambda is nonzero. The aggregate quotients are exactly A/(intersection Im phi_lambda) and A/(sum Im phi_lambda).
+
+If H_2(N;Z) ~= Z^beta, a family of subgroups K_lambda <= A is attainable by this construction iff each K_lambda is generated by at most beta elements. This is an existence theorem internal to the fixed finite nerve, constant band, Leray comparison, explicitly constructed non-split prestack, and broad typed-expansion convention. It is not an existence theorem for a lift canonically obtained from an empirical model or other pre-existing standard object.
+
+G. Main finite-group classification.
+
+Fix beta>=1 and a finite connected simplicial complex K_beta homotopy equivalent to the wedge of beta copies of S^2. For every m>=0 take the open-star cover of Sd^m K_beta. Let C_a be the pullback site generated by X_beta=|K_beta|, a strict initial empty object, and all nonempty finite intersections of all those covers, with the generated topology. These open-star covers are a filtered cofinal good-cover family satisfying H4 for every constant finite abelian band.
+
+For a finite abelian group G, the following are equivalent:
+
+   (i) G is nonzero, its minimum generator number d(G) is at most 2 beta, and G is not cyclic of order p^n for a prime p and n>=1;
+
+   (ii) within the broad typed-expansion class, there are a presheaf and a chosen non-split G-banded groupoid lift on that site with exactly two global components v_1,v_2, both component gerbes non-neutral, and
+
+       K_{v_1} intersection K_{v_2}=0,
+       K_{v_1}+K_{v_2}=G.
+
+When these conditions hold, Q_lab and the component-forgetting kernel are both isomorphic to G, Q_com=0, and matching without amalgamation holds. The proof combines the explicit construction above with the elementary finite-abelian criterion that G decomposes as G_1 direct-sum G_2, with both G_i nonzero and d(G_i)<=beta, iff d(G)<=2 beta and G is not a cyclic p-group. The statement is not claimed for bare empirical models.
+
+H. Exact limit of the empirical-model comparison.
+
+For a finite Abramsky--Brandenburger empirical model e with support presheaf S_e, compatible supported local families are naturally the same as global supported assignments and the terminally augmented sheafified sections. Thus strong contextuality is absence of a compatible family, not matching without amalgamation in the present sense. There is a canonical discrete zero-band stack Disc(aS_e), and after a band A is separately supplied there is a canonical split lift Disc(aS_e) x BA. Every component gerbe of that split lift is neutral, so every Giraud class and K_v is zero. These assignments are pseudonatural only for the context refinements explicitly specified in the paper. No component-section obstruction map with component sheaf aS_e can recover AMB classes for all finite models: a strongly contextual model has (aS_e)(X)=empty, so the H^2 component map has empty domain, while AMB's H^1 classes remain indexed by local sections and can be nonzero. Therefore the wedge classification transfers only after an extra band and non-split banded lift are supplied; it is not a classification of groups canonically attached to empirical models.
+
+MACHINE AND BUILD EVIDENCE
+
+The current generated files are a 48-page main.pdf and a 50-page supplement.pdf, both successfully compiled from the present sources. This is build evidence, not mathematical verification.
+
+There is no completed Lean formalization in the submission package. Although several TeX sources contain inert leanverified markers, the macro expands to nothing, no Lean proof artifacts are included, and the project's verification audit records 0/13 selected claims as Lean-verified. Do not treat those markers as evidence.
+
+A separate Python verifier from the preceding expert sprint was rerun on 2026-08-15, together with five unit tests; all five tests passed. It exhaustively checked, over its stated finite test domains: 6,561 instances of a sample continuous group 3-cocycle identity, 6,561 instances of the displayed NWW tau rewrite, 59,049 instances of the resulting Cech cocycle identity, 2,935 cyclic finite-quotient factorization cases, 3,896 three-subgroup exact-sequence cardinality cases for cyclic groups of modulus at most 36, and 160 beta-generator classification cases across seven small finite abelian groups. It also checks a strict pullback-image inclusion example and the following counterexample: lifting the conjugation action to a central extension does not by itself imply the second Peiffer identity, so it does not automatically produce a crossed module. These are finite/symbolic sanity checks, not proofs of the paper's general theorems. The verifier explicitly reports NWW Problems 8.1(b), 8.2(a), and 8.2(b) as open and unverified.
+
+THE ONE EXTERNAL ROUTE PREVIOUSLY IDENTIFIED
+
+You previously identified the explicit comparison between a crossed-module characteristic 3-class and the Cech 2-class of a lifting gerbe, through the Neeb--Wagemann--Wockel Cechization map tau, as the one named open route worth betting on, at roughly 45% probability of success. The precise reference is Neeb--Wagemann--Wockel, "Making Lifting Obstructions Explicit," Proc. London Math. Soc. 106 (2013), 589--620, Problem 8.1(b), DOI 10.1112/plms/pds047, arXiv:1108.5853. NWW ask whether
+
+   tau([c]) = +/- delta_1([G])
+
+in Cech H^2(Q,Z).
+
+One correction is essential. A lifted conjugation action on a central extension does not automatically make K-hat -> G a crossed module. Any serious restricted version must assume the strict crossed-module structure, including both Peiffer identities, or add an equivalent Peiffer compatibility condition. The present article's concluding remark now assumes that K-hat -> G is a crossed module and expressly says that the comparison is not proved.
+
+What this paper already supplies on the lifting-gerbe side is limited but concrete: the local objects, overlap isomorphisms, triple-overlap defect 2-cocycle, change by Cech coboundary, cover refinement, Cech-to-derived comparison when H4 holds, and transport of Giraud classes under compatible banded equivalence. What it does not supply is a chain model for locally continuous or locally smooth H^3, a representative formula for the crossed-module characteristic 3-cocycle, NWW's tau calculation in the same cover system as the lifting gerbe, the chain cochain/homotopy identifying the two representatives, determination of the sign, or the required naturality. The UCT image/cokernel results are only downstream consequences after such an identity has been established; they cannot establish it.
+
+Please now answer as a referee, not as a collaborator trying to preserve the project:
+
+1. With the two stated presentation grounds genuinely fixed, what is the best journal that would actually accept this paper on its present mathematics? Give a ranked list of named journals. For each journal, state the likely editorial/referee objection, the exact theorem-level or expository change it would demand, whether the 48+50-page split is acceptable, and your candid acceptance probability or qualitative likelihood. Distinguish journals that fit the actual contribution from journals that merely fit the vocabulary. If no reputable research journal is realistically likely to accept it, say so.
+
+2. Is the priority narrative above accurate? Treat this as the most important question. A referee on a sibling paper of ours recently found an omitted prior result that destroyed that paper's entire priority claim. Search conceptually beyond our vocabulary and name the nearest prior work for each result we call ours, especially: component-indexed gerbes of a stack with prescribed component sheaf; terminal-fibre rigidity or descent with strictly pullback-stable representatives; explicit cocycle prestacks realizing prescribed H^2 classes or evaluation-image subgroups; the intersection/sum aggregate quotients; and the wedge-of-spheres finite-group existence classification. Tell us which claims are genuinely new, which are standard lemmas in different notation, which appeared in our cited companion paper, and which have an omitted antecedent. For every priority objection, specify the citation and the smallest honest repair: cite/reframe, weaken, delete, or replace with a stronger theorem.
+
+3. Is the NWW comparison actually within reach of what THIS paper already builds, or would it require machinery that is substantially absent? Do not answer at the level of thematic similarity. Identify the exact missing chain-level objects and lemmas. If it is within reach, give the shortest honest proof route in a sharply delimited category, preferably finite-dimensional Lie groups with local smooth sections, finite discrete central band Z, a strict crossed module satisfying both Peiffer identities, and a common good cover or explicit common refinement. State exactly which existing results A--H above feed the proof, which do not, how the sign could be fixed rather than left as +/-, and what independence/naturality statements would still have to be proved. Also say whether completing that comparison belongs as an extension of this paper or necessarily as a separate paper.
+
+4. If the answer to question 3 is that the comparison is out of reach of the machinery actually present here, say so plainly and tell us to stop. Do not substitute a research program, a degree-shifted UCT theorem, another presentation-relative quotient, or a vague suggestion to learn the missing theory. Give the decisive stopping reason and the minimum result that would have had to be present for your answer to be different.
+
+End with one unambiguous referee verdict: submit now to your first-ranked journal; revise toward a specifically named journal with a finite list of mandatory changes; or stop this paper. Every objection must be paired with what would resolve it, and a hard rejection with reasons is preferred to a soft acceptance.
